## [2026-07-30] fix | ninth journal-critic pass (retried once after a spend-limit failure): a real data-leakage risk found in the two studies anchoring the classical-vs-deep-learning comparison

A ninth pass verified nine of Table 3's nineteen rows directly against primary sources (all reproduced correctly) and then found something more serious in the two it checked hardest: Wang et al. (2021) and Lee et al. (2018), the two highest classical/deep-learning accuracy figures the manuscript's method recommendation leans on, both have a real question mark over whether their cross-validation actually kept each participant's data on one side of the train/test split. Wang's own methods text describes "dividing all classes of gait data into four parts... and arranged them randomly" over 4,037 gait cycles from only 15 people, wording that reads as a per-cycle random split, not a per-participant one, verified directly against the source (confirmed again this round via a fresh fetch). The manuscript had stated, relying on a secondary source's account, that Wang's split was confirmed participant-level. Checked directly against Wang's own text, that specific claim doesn't hold up as stated. Both studies' Table 4 ratings were downgraded from an unqualified "Yes" to "Some concerns," with the specific textual basis stated.

Also fixed: the Conclusion implied head RMS passed the same pooled cross-dataset generalization test that trunk RMS and mean stride time did. It was never tested there at all, since only one of five pooled healthy datasets even has a head channel. Four locations conflated "sample entropy shows no effect within Felius" with "harmonic ratio reverses direction within Felius" as if both were the same kind of failure. They aren't, and the distinction matters since the weaker failure (no effect) applies to a feature 73% of the pooled stroke group depends on. The placement narrative's own numbers were checked precisely: foot RMS doesn't merely match lower-back RMS, it exceeds it in both datasets and ranks higher on the SNR check too, but foot RMS was never run through the actual pooled cross-dataset generalization test that earned trunk RMS its "most credible" label, since only three of five healthy datasets have any foot channel at all. Disclosed rather than left implicit. The Conclusion's closing "closer to converging than fragmented" characterization was checked against the review's own seven-feature tally and found to overstate it: only trunk RMS and mean stride time hold up cleanly across every check the review itself ran, and the other five each fail something. Reframed as "genuinely mixed" rather than converging.

One new reference added: the manuscript's own motivating premise (inter-rater variability in observational gait scoring) had no citation anywhere in the Introduction or Abstract. Found and verified a real, on-point 2025 source reporting inter-rater reliability for visual gait assessment as low as 0.04 to 0.59, added as a proper reference-list entry (a deliberate paragraph insertion, the first of this remediation, done by cloning an existing reference paragraph's direct formatting rather than assuming a default) and cited in Section 1.

**Deliberately deferred, given the scope of new investigation each would require**: (1) whether the "genuine trunk channel" subset's r = 0.91 result and Voisard's own cadence/mean-stride-time correlation of only -0.60 (expected closer to -1 given how the two are defined) point to a real computational inconsistency between the two timing estimators, flagged as an open question for the authors rather than resolved; (2) reporting absolute per-dataset descriptive statistics (means/SDs in converted units) for every Section 4.2 feature, which the review currently omits entirely for its own two real stroke datasets.

Document structure verified intact except for the one deliberate reference insertion: 261 paragraphs (260 plus one new reference), 7 tables, 4 inline shapes.


## [2026-07-30] fix | tenth journal-critic pass: a verified factual error that had been excluding the review's largest study from its own placement synthesis, and confirmation that Felius's cadence and stride time are deterministically linked (not independently measured, as claimed)

A tenth pass fetched Brasiliano et al. (2026), the largest paired stroke-and-healthy study in the whole review (85 vs. 97 participants), directly, twice, and found the manuscript's claim that it "does not report which placement produced which retained feature" is flatly wrong. The paper does report per-feature sensor attribution: of nine retained features, one is lower-back, two are shank, three are head-derived, one is sternum, one is a lower-back-to-head metric. This mattered doubly: the manuscript had used the false claim to exclude Brasiliano from the placement-comparison count in three locations, and the real result is directly relevant evidence on the review's last remaining placement claim (lower back beating head), since three of Brasiliano's nine retained features come from the head. Fixed in all three locations, with the added nuance that Brasiliano's own best-performing reduced model (three features, unsupervised) uses lower-back and shank, not head, so its own two analyses point in slightly different directions.

Separately, and independently, direct re-computation against the raw feature code confirmed something round 4 had gotten wrong from the start: the manuscript has claimed since round 4 that Felius, like Voisard, measures cadence and mean stride time independently. It doesn't. Felius ships no labeled gait events, so both quantities are derived from one shared autocorrelation frequency estimate, and their product is exactly 120 for every single Felius trial (confirmed directly: max deviation 1.4×10⁻¹⁴, floating-point noise). Only Voisard genuinely measures the two independently. A second consequence followed once this was checked properly: the manuscript's "Pearson r ≈ -0.60 across pooled trials" figure was actually just Voisard's own within-dataset correlation, mislabeled as the two-dataset pooled figure — the real pooled correlation, once Felius's near-deterministic pairs are genuinely included, is -0.80. Both Table 6 and Section 4.2.6 corrected to state this precisely.

Also propagated: the round-9 Wang/Lee-2018 data-leakage caveat, found in Table 4 only, had not reached the five other locations where those two studies' accuracy figures do argumentative work (Sections 4.1.3, 5.2, 5.3, 7, and the Conclusion) — fixed in all five, plus the held-out-evaluation count in Section 4.1.5 corrected from "fifteen of sixteen confirm cleanly" to state the two qualified cases explicitly rather than folding them into the clean count.

**Deliberately deferred, given the scope of new work each would require**: reporting absolute descriptive statistics (means/SDs in g) for accelerometer RMS across all seven datasets, which would let a reader check the pooled trunk-RMS result against the ~29% between-healthy-dataset spread the review's own numbers already show (DUO-GAIT ~1.02-1.04g vs. Camargo 1.33g); and adding Brasiliano's unsupervised-clustering arm (currently absent from Sections 2.2 and 4.1.3 entirely, despite being named in the review's own reference-list title) to the literature synthesis.

## [2026-07-31] fix | 13th journal-critic pass: three fatal code bugs found and fixed, WSL environment set up, full manuscript and notebook re-derivation
Round 13's journal-critic pass (upgraded protocol: exhaustive full-manuscript coverage, formatting/citation issues excluded, propagation-gap hunting) returned three FATAL findings, a first for this review-loop — not text/citation errors like rounds 1-12, but real bugs in the hands-on data-mining pipeline (`src/features/voisard.py`, `src/features/cross_dataset.py`). All three were independently re-verified against the actual code and data before any fix was made, per the project's standing verification discipline, then fixed and the entire empirical result re-derived from scratch. User explicitly approved the full recomputation and prose rewrite after reviewing the fatal findings.

**Bug 1 (cadence denominator)**: `voisard.py`'s cadence divided event counts by the recording's full length minus only the u-turn, silently counting idle recording time before/after the labeled walk as walking time, more severely for faster walkers. This had manufactured the review's flagship "cadence reverses after age-adjustment, only hands-on mining could catch this" finding — confirmed by hand-checking the two example trials the critic cited (matched the critic's own manual estimates to two decimal places). Fixed by computing the denominator from the actual span of labeled gait events minus the u-turn. Cadence is not an age artifact: corrected, it survives age-adjustment robustly (r = 0.44, p = 2.6e-13) in the correct clinical direction.

**Bug 2 (stride-time u-turn contamination)**: `_stride_time_stats` diffed all surviving gait-event timestamps sorted together, splicing one artificial multi-second "stride" spanning the entire u-turn into the mean/CV of every trial with a turn. This produced implausible stride-time-CV values (0.30-0.69 on a 10m walk). Fixed by computing diffs within the pre-turn and post-turn segments separately, never across the gap.

**Bug 3 (harmonic-ratio frequency mismatch, found this round while re-deriving the pooled comparison)**: `cross_dataset.py`'s `_row()` passed a step-rate frequency directly to `harmonic_ratio()` where the stride-rate fundamental was expected, for the three trunk-equivalent healthy-reference datasets computed via that default path (DUO-GAIT, MAREA, Camargo). Fixed by halving the frequency before passing it in. This flipped harmonic ratio's pooled-comparison result from "highly significant, r = -0.72" (bug-driven) to "not significant, r = 0.11, CI crosses zero" with a genuine cross-dataset direction disagreement (positive vs. Camargo/DUO-GAIT/MAREA, negative vs. OxWalk) disclosed instead.

**Sensor-hardware confound checked directly, not just disclosed**: Voisard's CVA cohort is 100% one accelerometer platform (TechnoConcept); its HS cohort mixes two (73% XSens, 27% TechnoConcept). A device-matched sensitivity check (TechnoConcept-only both arms) found every affected effect size held or strengthened relative to the mixed comparison — evidence the confound is real but not driving the reported findings.

**Stride-time variability's "opposite-sign cancellation" story is also gone** (both Voisard-alone and Felius-alone now agree in direction against the pooled healthy reference, r is approximately 0.46-0.49 each), but the pooled result is reported as unresolved rather than confirmed: the healthy reference mixes two incompatible stride-time-CV estimators (Voisard's own event-based calculation vs. a windowed-autocorrelation estimator used elsewhere), and even the estimator-matched subset shows one dataset (DUO-GAIT) returning a median 6-10x any other dataset using the identical method — a further inconsistency this round did not have the scope to resolve, disclosed rather than chased further.

**Environment note for future sessions**: `matio` (needed for Camargo's `.mat` files) is blocked on this machine by Smart App Control (an unmanaged-device Windows Code Integrity feature, confirmed via `Get-WinEvent -LogName Microsoft-Windows-CodeIntegrity/Operational`, Policy ID `{0283ac0f-fff1-49ae-ada1-8a933130cad6}`), not a true enterprise WDAC policy — this is a personal, unmanaged Windows 11 Home machine (confirmed via `dsregcmd /status`). Disabling Smart App Control is possible but semi-irreversible (cannot be re-enabled without a clean Windows reinstall) and was avoided. Instead, a WSL2 Ubuntu distribution was installed (`wsl --install -d Ubuntu`) with its own Python 3.11 venv at `/root/venv311` (`pip install mat-io numpy pandas scipy jupyter nbconvert ipykernel` — note the PyPI package name is `mat-io`, hyphenated, not `matio`), which runs outside Smart App Control's reach and can read the project's files directly via `/mnt/c/...`. A registered kernel `venv311fix` (absolute-path argv, avoids a PATH-resolution bug where the default `python3` kernelspec's bare `python` argv could resolve to the wrong interpreter via WSL's Windows-PATH interop) is needed to reliably `jupyter nbconvert --execute` the notebook — the default kernel silently produced stale results with zero errors. Full notebook re-execution (`notebooks/01_post_stroke_gait_baseline.ipynb`, including Camargo) takes about 10 minutes, longer than this session's default background-command timeouts — run it as one continuous `run_in_background` call at the 600000ms max, not detached/nohup'd (the WSL2 VM tears down when no `wsl.exe` session stays attached, killing detached background processes).

**Manuscript rewrite**: Sections 4.2.2 (age-adjustment), 4.2.6 (pooled cross-dataset comparison, full rewrite with bootstrap 95% CIs added throughout, addressing a separate round-13 finding that no CIs were reported anywhere in this section), the Abstract, Conclusion (5.1's convergence/disagreement discussion, Section 6 limitations, Section 8 conclusion), and the relevant future-direction item were all rewritten to match the corrected numbers. Foot RMS was formalized as a properly tracked feature (`foot_accel_rms_mean` added to `voisard.py`, age-adjusted r = 0.71 now verified via the same joint-OLS robustness check as the other features, resolving a round-13 finding that it had only ever been computed ad hoc). Also fixed this round: Rojek et al.'s unconfirmed sensor placement was removed from the deep-learning "clean vs. noisy placement" trade-off framing in P183/P215 (already fixed in the Abstract in round 12), the "8 to 144" deep-learning sample-size range was corrected (P184/P209, was conflating Wang's 8 supervised-classification participants with Felius's approximately 144 unsupervised-VAE total), Sun et al.'s internally inconsistent chronicity figure and missing sampling rate were flagged (Table 3/4, P124), Camargo's RMS statistic was clarified as RMS not mean (P159, reconciling the 1.03g vs. 1.33g figures as two different statistics of the same signal, not a discrepancy), and the asymmetric "at least as discriminative as" foot-vs-trunk hedging was corrected to "more discriminative than" in five locations to match Section 4.2.5's own finding.

## [2026-07-31] fix | 14th journal-critic pass: pooled-comparison validity findings, two more code bugs, trunk RMS substantially strengthened
Before this dispatch, per the newly-hardened precondition in the global `journal-critic` agent definition, the calling session ran `journal-format-check`, `academic-tone-check`, and `reviewer-neutrality-check` to a clean result first (fixed: 3 stray semicolons, an "actually"/"yet" pair, one dense comma-chain sentence, and one asymmetric-hedging verb in the Abstract). Round 14 then found the pooled cross-dataset comparison (Section 4.2.6) had deeper problems than round 13's three fatal bugs had fixed, plus several concrete self-contradictions this session's own round-13 rewrite had introduced. All findings independently re-verified against the code and manuscript text before any fix, per this project's standing discipline.

**Two more real code bugs found and fixed, on top of round 13's three:**
- **Windowing gap**: round 13 fixed cadence's denominator to exclude idle padding and the u-turn, but left lower-back/head/foot RMS, sample entropy, harmonic ratio, and Poincare SD1 in `voisard.py` reading the *entire* raw file, padding included. Fixed by computing explicit walking-only sample bounds (`_walking_bounds`/`_restrict_to_walking`) from the same labeled-event data cadence already uses, and restricting every raw-signal feature to them.
- **OxWalk RMS bug**: `cross_dataset.py`'s `_oxwalk_rows` computed lower-back RMS over OxWalk's entire free-living day-long recording, while `oxwalk.py`'s own documented convention restricts cadence and step-time CV to "active walking bouts" specifically to avoid this. Verified directly: only ~13% of a sample OxWalk file's rows fall inside an active bout. Fixed by adding `oxwalk.bout_mask()` and restricting the RMS computation to it, matching the existing cadence/step-time-CV convention.

**Net effect on the pooled comparison, all re-derived from scratch (Windows-native Voisard/Felius recomputation + WSL-based full 5-dataset pooled comparison with Camargo)**:
- Trunk RMS **strengthens substantially**: pooled r rises from 0.59 to **0.88** [0.82, 0.92], and it now separates the pooled stroke group from **every one of the five individual healthy-reference datasets significantly**, including OxWalk, which it previously failed against (that failure was itself the OxWalk bug, not a real placement-substitution effect as previously theorized).
- Sample entropy flips from spuriously "significant, contradicted by Felius's own internal null" to **cleanly non-significant everywhere** (pooled r = -0.04, p = .59, both Voisard-alone and Felius-alone against the pool also non-significant) — this resolves, in the cleanest possible way, a direct self-contradiction this session's own round-13 rewrite had left standing between P165 (called it credible) and P166 (correctly disqualified it).
- Age-adjusted Voisard RMS effects shift modestly: lower-back RMS 0.58→0.54, head RMS 0.30→0.32, foot RMS 0.71→0.72 (Felius's own RMS figures unaffected, since Felius has no documented equivalent padding issue and was not touched).
- Felius's foot RMS (r = 0.76, one of the paper's most consequential numbers, arguing foot beats trunk) turned out to have **no corresponding feature anywhere in `felius.py`** — implemented `foot_accel_rms_mean` there (parallel to Voisard's own fix) and the number checks out almost exactly at participant level (r = 0.761), so this was a reproducibility gap, not an error.

**Other concrete findings fixed:**
- A hardware confound (Voisard's CVA cohort is 100% one accelerometer platform, HS cohort mixes two) had been computed as a sensitivity check in an earlier round but **never actually written into the manuscript** — added to P137 with real numbers (every affected effect size holds or strengthens under a device-matched comparison, evidence against the confound explaining the findings).
- Table 6's "Mean stride time" row still carried the retracted r ≈ -0.60 / "independent measurement" claim that P165's prose had already corrected to r ≈ -0.96 — fixed.
- This session's own round-13 text miscounted which healthy-reference datasets share which stride-time-variability estimator ("four of five" when it's three, plus a fourth using a different estimator and a fifth computing nothing) — corrected.
- The pooled stride-time-variability result (r = +0.48) is **sign-reversed** relative to both real within-study effects (Voisard -0.26, Felius -0.77) — now stated explicitly rather than left for a reader to infer from the estimator-mismatch disclosure alone.
- Per-dataset trunk-RMS medians now disclosed directly in the manuscript (DUO-GAIT 1.03g, MAREA 1.12g, Camargo 1.25g, OxWalk 1.06g post-fix, GaitMotion 1.30g foot-substitute) so a reader can see the residual ~0.22g between-healthy-dataset spread for themselves, honestly flagged as real and larger than ideal for a single pooled magnitude estimate, rather than smoothed into one number — this was the critic's central "does this comparison even measure pathology" concern, and disclosure plus the two bug fixes above substantially answered it (the direction and significance are now robust against every individual dataset; the residual spread is a disclosed precision caveat, not a validity failure).
- The non-random dropout on cadence/stride-time features (161 of 182 stroke participants; missingness concentrated in Felius's own 17.5%-vs-0% stride-detection failure rate, already reported elsewhere in the manuscript but not connected to its effect on these specific features) is now disclosed in Section 4.2.6 directly.

## [2026-07-31] fix | Post-14th-pass cleanup: DUO-GAIT harmonic-ambiguity bug diagnosed and fixed, stride-time variability resolved, six minor findings fixed
Continuing directly from the 14th `journal-critic` pass, working through its remaining minor findings and one further self-investigation before considering the manuscript ready for a next review round.

**A fourth code bug found and fixed, this time self-identified rather than critic-flagged**: while investigating the round-14 log's own open item ("the DUO-GAIT stride-time-CV anomaly noted above was disclosed but not diagnosed"), direct inspection of DUO-GAIT's per-subject cadence values showed a clear bimodal pattern (roughly half the subjects at ~50-56 steps/min, implausibly slow, the rest at ~100-120, normal). Traced to the autocorrelation-based frequency detector: DUO-GAIT's sacral placement on highly symmetric healthy gait produces two comparably strong autocorrelation peaks, one at the true step period and one at exactly double it (the stride period), confirmed directly by inspecting several subjects' own autocorrelation arrays (the "wrong" cases showed a peak at half the detected lag with 94-99.5% of the top peak's strength; the "correct" cases showed no such competing peak at all). Fixed with two additions to `src/features/signal_utils.py`, both opt-in (default `disambiguate_harmonic=False`, so every other dataset's existing behavior is completely unaffected): (1) `dominant_stride_frequency` now checks for a comparably-strong peak at half the detected lag and prefers it when present, (2) `windowed_stride_time_cv` additionally filters out per-window estimates deviating more than 40% from the window set's own median, needed because short 5-second windows could also lock onto 1/3 or 1/4 sub-harmonics the simple half-lag check doesn't catch. Only `cross_dataset.py`'s `_duogait_rows()` opts into this.

**Result**: DUO-GAIT's own cadence values are now uniformly plausible (101-120 steps/min), and its stride-time CV drops from an implausible 0.20-0.53 range to 0.05-0.10, matching every other dataset's scale. Re-deriving the full pooled comparison with this fix:
- Cadence strengthens further: r = 0.47 → **0.57** [0.45, 0.68].
- Mean stride time strengthens further: r = -0.45 → **-0.55** [-0.67, -0.43].
- Both now separate the pooled stroke group from **every individual healthy dataset with no exception** — the previously-disclosed "unexplained failure against DUO-GAIT specifically" for these two features is gone, because that failure *was* the bug.
- **Stride-time variability is now resolved rather than merely disclosed as unresolved.** Restricted to only the datasets sharing Voisard's/Felius's own windowed-autocorrelation estimator family (Felius, DUO-GAIT, MAREA, Camargo), the effect **vanishes entirely** (r = -0.03 [-0.24, 0.17], p = .76, medians 0.054 vs. 0.059). The full pool's nominal significance (r = 0.35) is now cleanly attributable to OxWalk's differently-defined, annotation-based step-time CV (median 0.29, four to five times every other dataset's own value), not a genuine stroke-versus-healthy signal. This is read as a null finding once measured consistently, not an open question.

**Six further minor findings from round 14's report, fixed**:
1. P122's study-count claim ("twelve of 16... with no qualification") was off by one and missing Inui et al. (2026) from its own exception list — Table 4 has 11 unqualified "Yes" rows, not 12; corrected, Inui added alongside Wang and Lee (2018).
2. Section 4.2.5/Conclusion stated "only three of the five" pooled healthy datasets provide a foot or ankle channel — checked against Table 5 directly: it's four of five (all but OxWalk). Corrected, and reframed accurately: the limiting factor is that this review's own extraction code doesn't currently compute a foot RMS feature for the healthy-reference datasets, not that most lack the channel.
3. Hsu et al. (2018)'s placement count was inconsistent: "four" in two locations (one explicitly marked "verified against the original text" in an earlier round) versus "five" in a third. Corrected the outlier to four.
4. The SNR feature ranking (Section 4.2.4) is computed on raw, not age-adjusted, Voisard features — confirmed directly against the notebook's own cell — and this wasn't stated. Added a sentence noting the ranking should be read alongside the age-correlation finding, since the RMS placements dominating the ranking are also the features most correlated with age.
5. MAREA's own contribution to the pooled comparison mixes two protocols (subjects 1-11 from one timing table, 12-20 from another) without disclosure in Section 4.2.6, even though the resulting ~9-10 steps/min cadence difference is already reported elsewhere in the manuscript for a within-MAREA comparison. Added a cross-reference disclosing this applies to the pooled comparison too.
6. Camargo's anatomical landmark remains unconfirmed after a further, differently-angled search attempt (now six-plus attempts across rounds) — treated as genuinely unresolvable from available sources rather than re-attempted further, consistent with how it was already disclosed.

## [2026-07-31] fix | Round 16: DUO-GAIT raw-file bug (fatal), MAREA/Camargo harmonic-ambiguity extension, sample-entropy resampling confound, Voisard participant-level correction, SNR ranking fix

Working through round 15's `journal-critic` report (six Major concerns, eleven Minor, seven Questions) directly rather than dispatching another pass, per the user's explicit instruction. Verified every claim against primary sources/direct code inspection before fixing, consistent with this project's standing discipline, rather than trusting the critic's or this log's own prior framing at face value.

**M1 (fatal, confirmed via primary source)**: DUO-GAIT's raw `repository_raw/OG_st_raw/sub_XX/SA.csv` files, read directly by `_duogait_rows()`, are NOT walking trials. Confirmed via direct file inspection (header: "Total data points: 376256" at 128 Hz ≈ 49 minutes) and independently via Zhou et al. (2023), Scientific Data, PMC10442385: "Raw IMU data ... were continuously recorded from the start of the first walking session to the end of the second walking session" and "the 'OG_st_raw' folder contains the data from the entire single task visits (i.e., ST-Control and ST-Fatigue)." The file spans a 6-minute control walk, a non-walking fatigue-inducing protocol, and a 6-minute fatigue walk concatenated, with no usable per-sample walking annotation (the raw export's own "Event" column is an empty placeholder). Every raw-signal feature (RMS, cadence, sample entropy, harmonic ratio, Poincare SD1) had been computed over this whole ~49-minute mix, diluted by ~43 minutes of non-walking content. The round-14 harmonic-disambiguation fix was patching a symptom of this, not the cause. Fixed by switching `duogait.py`/`cross_dataset.py` to the dataset's own already-segmented `repository_interim/OG_st_control` files (confirmed 48502 rows ≈ 6.3 minutes, matching the paper's stated protocol), with a new small parser for their simpler CSV format.

**M2**: MAREA and Camargo were already properly windowed at the loader level (unlike DUO-GAIT), but direct testing showed both shared DUO-GAIT's step/stride harmonic-ambiguity bug in `windowed_stride_time_cv`: enabling `disambiguate_harmonic=True` dropped MAREA's mean stride-time-CV from 0.120 (exactly matching round 15's flagged discrepancy) to 0.047, and Camargo's from 0.067 to 0.026, both now in line with DUO-GAIT. Extended the flag to both as the new default. This also surfaced a second, independent bug in the disambiguation logic itself: the accepted half-lag candidate wasn't bounds-checked against `min_lag`, so it could select a candidate faster than the function's own stated physiological ceiling — confirmed directly (Camargo trial AB10: `min_lag=80`, accepted `half_lag=46`), producing a few implausible cadences (~260-286 steps/min) instead of corrected ones. Fixed with a `min_lag` floor on the accepted half-lag; re-verified afterward that the only remaining cadence corrections (Camargo subject AB12, two trials) moved from an implausibly slow ~58-61 steps/min to a plausible ~117-122, consistent with a genuine fix rather than a new artifact.

**M3**: `sample_entropy()`'s fixed-sample-count cap (1500, via uniform index downsampling) created an undisclosed, severe cross-dataset confound: datasets with longer native recordings ended up computed at a much coarser effective temporal resolution than short ones. Confirmed directly: DUO-GAIT/MAREA's ~6-10 minute walking segments landed at ~4 Hz effective resolution, while Camargo's ~11-37 second trials stayed near-native at ~93 Hz, a ~20-25x spread. This plausibly explained most of Camargo's (and GaitMotion's) much lower raw sample-entropy values relative to DUO-GAIT/MAREA/OxWalk, rather than a genuine gait-regularity difference. Fixed by resampling every signal to a common 10 Hz effective rate (`TARGET_SAMPEN_HZ`) before computing entropy, using `fs` as a new required parameter (added a `sampen_fs` override in `cross_dataset._row()` for OxWalk, whose cadence/stride-CV come from annotation overrides with `fs=None` but which still has a confirmed native rate for entropy specifically; GaitMotion now correctly returns NaN, since its own sampling rate remains unconfirmed, rather than silently comparing an unresampled signal). Also fixed, while touching this code path: `voisard.py`'s `_channel_nonlinear_features` had hardcoded `fs=100.0` for its harmonic-ratio call regardless of the trial's actual recorded rate — checked directly and found every sampled trial's own `meta.json` does report 100.0 regardless of TechnoConcept vs. XSens hardware, so this hadn't produced a wrong value in practice, but now reads the trial's own field.

**Consequence of M1+M2+M3 together on the pooled comparison (Section 4.2.6)**: trunk RMS strengthens further, r = 0.88 → **0.91** [0.86, 0.95]. Cadence and mean stride time are essentially unchanged (r = 0.57, r = -0.56). Stride-time variability becomes a **clean, direct null** in the full pool (r = -0.01 [-0.18, 0.18], p = .91) once the disambiguation fix is applied consistently across all three trunk-equivalent datasets — no longer needs the "restricted estimator family" subset workaround earlier rounds relied on to see the null. Sample entropy remains non-significant overall (r = -0.10 [-0.23, 0.04]), but a deeper per-stroke-dataset check found something an earlier round's "clean disqualification, both non-significant" framing missed: Voisard-alone and Felius-alone are each individually **significant against the pooled healthy reference**, but in **opposite directions** (Voisard r = -0.83, Felius r = 0.18) — a genuine sign-cancellation, not a uniform absence of effect, and read as more concerning than the flat non-significant summary implied. **Harmonic ratio flips to significant**, r = 0.17 [0.01, 0.32], p = .022, reversing the earlier "not significant, genuine cross-dataset direction disagreement (positive vs. Camargo/DUO-GAIT/MAREA, negative vs. OxWalk)" reading — all four computable datasets now agree on direction; the earlier disagreement was itself an artifact of the same bugs M1/M2 fixed. Checked further, this pooled significance rests specifically on Voisard's own contribution (r = 0.45) — Felius, which supplies 73% of the pooled stroke group, shows no effect at all (p = .55) — so it is read as suggestive, not equally credible alongside trunk RMS/cadence/mean stride time. A further, previously unqualified "no exception" claim for cadence and mean stride time against every individual healthy dataset also needed correcting: OxWalk's own comparison narrowly misses conventional significance for both (p = .051, p = .069), the only healthy-reference dataset where either fails to clear the bar individually.

**M4**: Voisard's Section 4.2.2 age-adjustment had always run at the trial level (128 CVA / 360 HS trials) while directly-compared Felius numbers in the same sentences ran at participant level, contradicting the review's own stated participant-level principle (already applied in Section 4.2.6). Confirmed the inconsistency was real and consequential: Voisard's own trial counts run as high as 16 per CVA participant and 29 per HS participant. Re-run at the participant level (49 CVA / 73 HS): five of six features remain significant with broadly similar effect sizes to the trial-level figures (lower back RMS 0.54→0.57, foot RMS 0.72→0.72, head RMS 0.32→0.31, cadence 0.44→0.42, mean stride time -0.44→-0.42). Stride-time variability is the exception — it now falls short of significance under the primary bootstrap test (r = -0.18 [-0.38, 0.04], p = .097), though a joint OLS robustness check still finds its group coefficient significant (p = .0022), a genuine method-dependent discordance disclosed rather than resolved in either direction. The device-matched (TechnoConcept-only) sensitivity check was also re-run at participant level (49 CVA / 20 HS, was 128/99 trials): here stride-time variability comes back significant (r = -0.37, p = .016), the smaller but device-homogeneous subset resolving its own participant-level ambiguity in the significant direction.

**M6**: P154's SNR ranking recomputed properly. The notebook's own `LITERATURE_FEATURE_COLUMNS` never included foot RMS despite P154 claiming a 9-candidate ranking; with foot RMS genuinely added and the M3 sample-entropy fix applied, the correct ranking is foot RMS (1st) > sample entropy (2nd, now ahead of lower-back RMS itself) > lower-back RMS (3rd) > head RMS (4th) > mean stride time (5th) > cadence (6th) > harmonic ratio (7th, **not** lowest as previously claimed) > stride-time CV (8th) > Poincare SD1 (9th, actually lowest).

**Q7 (modeling.py)**: confirmed `src/features/modeling.py` (SVM/RF baseline classifiers, sequential backward feature selection, nested subject-grouped CV, k-medoids clustering) is genuinely orphaned dead code — not imported by the notebook or `cross_dataset.py` anywhere, and this log's own round-14 entry already documents the notebook's classifier-generalization cells and `cross_dataset.py`'s wrapper function being deliberately removed when the review's scope was narrowed; the `modeling.py` file itself was simply never deleted afterward. No results from it ever reached the manuscript. Deleted the file, consistent with this project's own precedent for orphaned code contradicting a stated scope decision.

**Minor fixes**: P157's "nine-sensor, six-site" DUO-GAIT placement comparison corrected to "seven-sensor, five-site" (checked directly against `duogait.py`'s own `SENSORS`/`SENSOR_LABELS` — 7 sensor labels across 5 anatomical sites, wrist and foot each bilateral). ## [2026-07-31] fix | Post-round-16 pattern audit: Felius checked and deliberately left alone, one DUO-GAIT sensor-count inconsistency, 25 comma-density/casual-connective fixes in round 16's own new prose

Direct follow-up to round 16, per the user's explicit request to (1) apply the round's bug patterns across every remaining dataset before considering it closed, and (2) check the round's own newly-written prose for register/formatting flaws, since it was written quickly across many small substitutions without an intervening tone check.

**Dataset pattern audit**: Tested every dataset not yet checked for M1/M2/M3-style bugs.
- **Felius** (73% of pooled stroke data — the highest-stakes remaining check): its foot-channel single-estimate frequency detection is provably unaffected by the step/stride harmonic ambiguity (0 of 762 signals changed when `disambiguate_harmonic` was toggled), consistent with the established biomechanical reasoning that a foot/ankle sensor's autocorrelation peak is unambiguous, unlike a trunk sensor's. Its *windowed* stride-time-CV outlier filter, however, does change materially (125 of 724 windows, mean diff larger in Stroke trials than Healthy: 21.4% vs. 4.9%). Direct inspection of the underlying per-window autocorrelation values found a real, important difference from DUO-GAIT/MAREA/Camargo: the flagged Healthy-trial windows show the clean signature of a genuine measurement artifact (a single stride-time value at almost exactly double the surrounding cluster, positive half-lag value), but the flagged Stroke-trial windows show low autocorrelation strength throughout (0.1-0.4, vs. DUO-GAIT's clean ~0.84+ cases) and *negative* half-lag values for the slow-stride windows — the opposite signature from a harmonic-lock artifact, and consistent instead with genuine, severe stride-to-stride irregularity, an expected feature of impaired gait rather than noise. Applying the same outlier filter here risks discarding real pathological signal rather than correcting a bug. **Decision: left Felius's stride-time-CV computation unchanged**, a considered judgment call rather than an oversight, given the asymmetric risk (healthy population: the filter only removes genuine bugs; stroke population: it would also remove some genuine variability).
- **GaitMotion**: already correctly excluded from every M1-M2-style windowing/disambiguation risk, since its cadence/harmonic ratio were never computed at all (unconfirmed sampling rate) and its `.pkl` files bundle each trial's own raw signal with its own per-stride parameter table, unlike DUO-GAIT's mismatched raw-vs-processed split. Not reproduced by the same bug pattern.
- **Voisard**: its cadence and stride-time-CV are event-based, not autocorrelation-based, and were never at risk from M2. Its raw-signal windowing (M1-style) was already fixed in an earlier round (`_walking_bounds`).
- **MAREA, Camargo, OxWalk, DUO-GAIT**: already fixed in round 16 itself.

**One real cross-paragraph inconsistency found while auditing**: P157 was corrected this round to "seven-sensor, five-site" for DUO-GAIT's placement comparison, but P159 still referenced "DUO-GAIT's independent nine-sensor comparison" when describing the same comparison from Camargo's side — fixed to match.

**Writing-quality check**: ran `academic-tone-check` against every paragraph and table cell edited in round 16 (P137, P154, P157, P159, P165, P166, P213, P215, P216, Table 6's SampEn row). Found 25 real violations of this project's own 3-comma-per-sentence ceiling (several sentences carried 4-9 non-list commas, the densest being two 60-90-word sentences in P165 and P157 built from multiple stacked asides), plus two casual mid-sentence "so" connectives left over from pre-round-16 phrasing that a new edit sat directly next to. Fixed all 25 by splitting into shorter sentences at the point the logical relationship changes (per the skill's stated preference over deleting commas and running clauses together) or, for the two "so" instances, restructuring within one sentence using an embedded "and ... therefore" rather than a fresh split. Re-verified zero semicolons remain anywhere in the document, including table cells, and structural integrity is unchanged (261 paragraphs, 7 tables, 4 inline shapes).

## [2026-07-31] fix | Full cross-dataset bug-pattern re-audit: two more dormant/live bugs found (Voisard/OxWalk segment concatenation), three orphaned functions sharing the round-13 harmonic-ratio bug deleted, one missing unit conversion fixed

Direct follow-up to the post-round-16 pattern audit, per the user's explicit request to re-check every bug ever found across all 15+ rounds against every dataset, not just the one or two it was originally found in. Went through every historical bug class (raw-signal windowing, unit conversion, cadence step/stride-multiplier convention, harmonic-ratio frequency convention, sample-entropy downsampling, autocorrelation harmonic ambiguity, trial- vs. participant-level aggregation) and checked each against all seven datasets directly, rather than trusting an earlier round's "this dataset's own loader already restricts to walking segments" note without re-verifying.

**Confirmed clean, verified directly rather than assumed**: MAREA's and Camargo's raw-signal loaders restrict to genuinely walking-only segments (MAREA's timing-table-defined treadWalk/indoorWalk/outdoorWalk segments range 175-607 s; Camargo's trial windows range 11-37 s), not whole-session recordings — no DUO-GAIT-style M1 bug. Felius's own trial files range 0-207 s (median 143 s), consistent with single walking trials, one degenerate 0-sample file found (1 of 381, handled gracefully by existing NaN-guards, immaterial). GaitMotion's raw-vs-labeled-stride ratio looks suspiciously large, but its true sampling rate remains unconfirmable from available data (no sample-index column links strides to raw positions), and the manuscript already discloses this and demonstrates every finding is unchanged with GaitMotion excluded entirely — flagged as inconclusive-but-immaterial rather than chased further.

**Found and fixed: three dead functions sharing round 13's original harmonic-ratio bug.** Round 13 fixed a step-vs-stride frequency-convention bug (harmonic_ratio needs the stride fundamental, not the step rate a trunk-equivalent sensor's `dominant_stride_frequency` actually returns) in `cross_dataset.py`'s shared `_row()`, and a matching cadence x1-vs-x2 multiplier convention. Grepping for every other call site of `dominant_stride_frequency`/`harmonic_ratio` found the identical pair of bugs, never retrofitted, in three standalone functions: `duogait.build_task_classification_table`, `marea.build_environment_classification_table`, `camargo.build_locomotion_classification_table`. Checked whether any of the three is actually reachable from the notebook or any other code path — none is; all three are leftovers from the same abandoned RQ3 pipeline-generalization investigation this log's round-12 entries already document removing from the notebook and from `cross_dataset.py`'s `classifier_generalization_check`, just never deleted from their own dataset modules. Deleted all three (and their now-orphaned companion constants `TASK_FEATURE_COLUMNS`, `ENVIRONMENT_FEATURE_COLUMNS`, `camargo.FEATURE_COLUMNS`/`MODES`/`LITERATURE_PLACEMENT`, and the now-unused `signal_utils` imports in each file), consistent with this project's own precedent for orphaned code contradicting a stated scope (`modeling.py`, deleted round 16) — the bug is real but the code is unreachable, so removing it is safer than patching code nobody exercises.

**Found and fixed: a missing unit conversion.** `cross_dataset.harmonize_voisard_features` converts Voisard's `lb_accel_rms` and `he_accel_rms` from m/s^2 to g before pooling against Felius (already in g), but never converted `foot_accel_rms_mean`. Checked whether this had produced a wrong number anywhere: no — foot RMS is currently only compared via rank-biserial effect size within Voisard's own native units (unit-invariant), never against another dataset's raw foot-RMS magnitude directly, since P157/P213 already disclose foot RMS was never entered into the pooled cross-dataset comparison. A dormant bug, not a live one, but one that would silently corrupt a future fix to that exact gap. Added `foot_accel_rms_mean` to the conversion loop.

**Found and fixed, the most consequential finding this pass: a segment-concatenation confound affecting Voisard's own sample entropy, harmonic ratio, and Poincare SD1, on top of OxWalk's already-known version of the same bug.** OxWalk's `bout_mask`-based RMS restriction (round 14) is safe for RMS (order-independent) but was never safe for sample entropy or harmonic ratio, both of which assume genuine sample-to-sample continuity (windowed autocorrelation, FFT) — concatenating a typical OxWalk file's 22-54 separate walking bouts (confirmed directly on a 5-file sample) via boolean-mask indexing introduces that many artificial discontinuities into the signal. This had been identified as an open concern earlier in this session but never actually fixed. While checking whether Voisard's own `_restrict_to_walking` (pre-turn plus post-turn straight-walking segments, joined with `pd.concat`) shared the same risk: confirmed directly that **every single one of 1356 checked trials** has both a pre-turn and a post-turn segment, meaning every trial's sample entropy/harmonic ratio/Poincare SD1 computation has run on a signal with exactly one artificial discontinuity at the stitch point, in 100% of trials, not an edge case. `harmonic_ratio`'s FFT-based computation is specifically vulnerable to this (a single discontinuity anywhere in the array introduces broadband spectral leakage across every frequency via the Gibbs phenomenon, confirmed by reading its own implementation), and Poincare SD1 computes literal sample-to-sample differences, making the stitch point itself one of the differenced pairs.

Fixed both the same way: added `oxwalk.bout_bounds()` (returns bout boundaries as index ranges rather than a flat mask) and rewrote `cross_dataset._oxwalk_rows()` to compute sample entropy and harmonic ratio per bout (bouts below a 120-sample/1.2 s floor excluded, matching `sample_entropy`'s own minimum after the round-16 common-rate resampling) and combine via a sample-count-weighted average, rather than on the concatenated array; RMS is left on the concatenated array since it is unaffected. Symmetrically rewrote `voisard._channel_nonlinear_features` to compute sample entropy, harmonic ratio, and Poincare SD1 per bound (pre-turn, post-turn) and weighted-average, rather than on `_restrict_to_walking`'s single concatenated array; RMS is unaffected and left as-is.

**Net effect, re-derived from scratch**: trunk RMS, cadence, mean stride time, and stride-time variability are all completely unchanged (none of these features touch sample entropy, harmonic ratio, or Poincare SD1). Sample entropy's pooled effect weakens further toward a clean null, r = -0.10 [-0.23, 0.04] -> **r = -0.04 [-0.17, 0.10]**, consistent with the concatenation artifact having added spurious noise rather than a genuine cross-dataset signal. The Voisard-alone/Felius-alone sign-cancellation story (opposite-sign, both individually significant) survives, with updated magnitudes: Voisard r = -0.83 -> **-0.88** (the artifact had been diluting a genuine effect, not manufacturing a spurious one, since removing it strengthened Voisard's own signal), Felius r = 0.18 -> **0.27** (Felius itself was untouched by either fix; this shift reflects the pooled healthy reference's own entropy distribution changing once OxWalk's fix is applied). Harmonic ratio is essentially unchanged, r = 0.17 -> 0.17 (0.168), p = .022 -> .024, still marginally significant, Voisard-alone r = 0.45 -> 0.47, Felius-alone still not significant (p = .64). Manuscript updated in P165 (three sentences) and Table 6 (SampEn and Harmonic Ratio rows, disclosing the fix and its FFT-specific mechanism); P166 and P215 restate this split only qualitatively and needed no numeric change. Notebook re-executed end to end after all fixes. Structural integrity unchanged throughout (261 paragraphs, 7 tables, 4 inline shapes).

**Consciously not pursued further**: GaitMotion's raw-signal duration relative to its own labeled-stride count remains unverifiable without a confirmed sampling rate or a sample-index-to-stride mapping, neither available from the dataset's public release; the manuscript's own existing robustness check (excluding GaitMotion changes nothing) is treated as sufficient given this, rather than pursuing an unresolvable question further.

The cited numbers (r = 0.76, 0.59) happen to already be the correct participant-level figures — likely computed correctly at some earlier point and then given a wrong justification — so only the false claim needed fixing, not the numbers themselves.

**Confirmed real, deliberately disclosed rather than corrected — two duration/resolution confounds in `harmonic_ratio` (Table 6)**: (1) the per-bout floor added this round for OxWalk's segment-concatenation fix reused sample entropy's own minimum bout length (1.2 s) for harmonic ratio too, even though harmonic ratio's frequency resolution needs a longer window to reliably separate adjacent harmonics — fixed with a separate, longer floor (10 s) for harmonic ratio specifically. (2) The deeper issue — `harmonic_ratio` reads one FFT bin per harmonic from a single transform of the whole input, so its frequency resolution varies by more than two orders of magnitude across the pooled datasets' trial durations (roughly 0.002 Hz on the longest walks to 0.03-0.5 Hz on the shortest Camargo/OxWalk bouts) — was judged too large a redesign to implement and fully re-verify in this round, and is disclosed directly in Table 6's Harmonic Ratio row instead.

**Confirmed real, reframed rather than just relabeled — the sample-entropy "sign-cancellation" story was over-read**: checked whether Felius's own healthy participants also differ from the pooled healthy reference on sample entropy (a between-dataset baseline-offset check) — they do, r = 0.31, p = .007, on the same feature. Felius's "significant effect against the pooled reference" (r = 0.27) is therefore better read as reflecting this baseline shift (present in both its stroke and healthy groups) rather than a second, genuine, opposite-signed pathology signal to set against Voisard's own real effect — consistent with Felius's own within-study comparison (stroke vs. its own matched controls) showing no effect at all. P165 rewritten to state this precisely rather than the previous "two real effects that happen to disagree in sign" framing, which the evidence does not support as stated.

**A confirmed false positive in the critic's own report, caught by direct re-verification rather than accepted on the critic's authority**: the critic's M2 finding claimed P141's Felius corroboration figures (r = -0.63, -0.77, 0.59) were stale trial-level numbers. Independent recomputation at the participant level gives r = -0.629, -0.769, 0.587 — rounding to exactly the numbers already in the manuscript. P141 was not touched; the critic appears to have quoted from a different, trial-level notebook cell than the one the manuscript's own prose actually reflects. Recorded here so this specific finding is not reopened without a fresh check.

**Code fixes**: `marea.py`'s own `build_feature_table()`/`_segment_features()` never received the `disambiguate_harmonic` flag `cross_dataset._marea_rows()` already uses for MAREA's Waist sensor, so the two computed different stride-time-CV values for the identical signal — fixed (scoped to the Waist/step-periodic case only, confirmed not to change the LF-sensor default the notebook actually calls, so no manuscript number needed updating). `cross_dataset._oxwalk_rows()`'s harmonic-ratio bout floor separated from sample entropy's, as above.

Full notebook re-executed after all fixes (5m45s, zero errors). Structural integrity confirmed unchanged (261 paragraphs, 7 tables, 4 inline shapes) throughout, zero semicolons. Pre-existing mandatory tone/comma-density check applied to every newly-written sentence this round, two violations found and fixed (P93, P155).

**Consciously deferred, stated explicitly rather than silently dropped**: a full redesign of `harmonic_ratio` to use a fixed-duration window or per-harmonic bandwidth integration, which would remove the resolution confound at its source rather than only disclosing it — judged too large a change to implement and fully re-verify against a fresh notebook re-derivation within this round's scope.

## [2026-08-01] review+fix | Round 18 journal-critic: 12 findings, all independently re-verified against live code/notebook/manuscript before acting — 9 confirmed and fixed, 1 confirmed as already-resolved (no new action), 2 disclosed rather than corrected given scope

Round 18's `journal-critic` report disclosed upfront that it could not open the `.docx` this round and worked entirely from code, notebook, and `numbers-registry.md`, which meant every finding needed independent verification against the live manuscript before acting, not just the code, this project's own established discipline for this exact situation.

**M1, confirmed and the most consequential finding this round, stride-time-variability's "clean null" is an OxWalk estimator artifact, not a genuine absence of effect**: OxWalk's stride-time CV is an annotation-derived, whole-day step-time CV pooled across 20-54 separate walking bouts at different speeds, a fundamentally different estimator from the other four healthy datasets' windowed-autocorrelation period-estimate CV, and runs 5-12x higher as a result (mean 0.27 vs. 0.02-0.05). Confirmed directly: excluding OxWalk and re-running the five-dataset pooled test against the remaining four converts r = -0.01 [-0.18, 0.18], p = .91 into r = -0.65 [-0.78, -0.50], p = 1.15e-13, one of the strongest effects in the whole comparison. The manuscript's pre-round-18 text had explicitly argued the null held even "without needing to exclude OxWalk," a claim this round's direct check found does not hold. Fixed in P165, P213, P215, P216, plus a new reproducible notebook cell rather than only an asserted prose claim.

**M2, confirmed, round 16's harmonic-ratio "all four datasets agree on direction" claim was never actually checked against OxWalk's own per-dataset mean**: checked directly this round. Camargo (4.59), DUO-GAIT (7.48), and MAREA (5.95) all sit above the pooled stroke median (2.48), but OxWalk's own mean (1.54) sits below it, the genuine cross-dataset direction disagreement the pre-round-16 manuscript had originally and correctly disclosed. Round 16's "resolution" was itself the error. Fixed in P165 (two separate locations within the same paragraph, the claim was restated twice), P166, P215, P216.

**M3/M4, confirmed and fixed in the notebook, not the manuscript, which already had the correct numbers from a separate, correct computation**: Voisard's own round-16 participant-level re-ranking cell ended in a bare `pd.DataFrame(rows)` never assigned to a variable, so the notebook's own downstream placement-ranking cells kept silently reading a stale trial-level table. Felius had no participant-level re-ranking cell in the notebook at all, only ever computed ad hoc in scratch scripts, never landed as a real cell. `foot_accel_rms_mean` was missing from both datasets' placement maps, so foot could not structurally win the ranking even though it is the strongest feature by both effect size and SNR. All four fixed. Checked whether this notebook bug had also corrupted the manuscript: it had not, P157/P213/P214/P216 already state foot beats lower back for both real stroke datasets, from an earlier round's separately-correct computation, confirmed to match the freshly re-verified numbers (Voisard r = 0.719 vs. manuscript's 0.72, Felius r = 0.760 vs. manuscript's 0.76) almost exactly.

**M5, confirmed and fixed, P154's SNR ranking was correct when written but never re-checked after a later fix changed the underlying numbers**: sample entropy's F-ratio is now 209.5 (3rd place, behind lower-back RMS's 268.9), not the 306.4 that had it ranked 2nd, ahead of lower-back RMS. Fixed in P154 and the registry's SNR table, with an explicit correction note rather than a silent number swap.

**M6, confirmed and fixed, the pooled harmonic-ratio numbers had drifted since round 17's own `MIN_BOUT_SAMPLES_FOR_HR` fix and were never re-verified downstream**: current values are r = 0.18 [0.03, 0.33], p = .015, n = 161 stroke / 95 healthy, against the manuscript's stated r = 0.17, p = .022-.024. Fixed in P165, registry.

**M9, confirmed and fixed at the code level, `poincare_sd()` had the identical sampling-rate confound already fixed for `sample_entropy()` in round 16, but was itself never fixed**: SD1 is computed from consecutive-sample differences, so for a band-limited signal it scales with native sampling rate rather than gait regularity alone, exactly the mechanism M3 already fixed for sample entropy. `gaitmotion.py`'s own docstring for `build_pathology_classification_table()` directly and falsely claimed "only sampling-rate-independent features are used here (RMS, sample entropy, Poincare SD1)" two lines above a call passing `fs=None` to `sample_entropy()` specifically because it is not rate-independent, a literal self-contradiction. Fixed by adding a required `fs` parameter to `poincare_sd()` (all four call sites updated, `voisard.py:203`, `felius.py:100`, `gaitmotion.py:108`, `cross_dataset.py:134`), resampling to the same `TARGET_SAMPEN_HZ` sample entropy already uses, and correcting the false docstring claim. GaitMotion's own SD1 value now correctly returns NaN (fs unconfirmed) rather than a spurious value computed at an uncontrolled native rate, consistent with its existing cadence/harmonic-ratio treatment. Table 6's Poincare SD1 row updated with the same disclosure style already used for sample entropy's own row.

**M10, confirmed and disclosed rather than corrected, a residual sample-entropy duration confound survives the round-16 rate fix**: per-dataset healthy sample-entropy means still span roughly a 2.6-fold range (Camargo ~0.51, DUO-GAIT ~0.98, MAREA ~1.11, OxWalk ~1.30) despite all reflecting similar healthy overground gait, because per-dataset segment duration itself is not harmonized (an ~11 s Camargo trial vs. several-minute DUO-GAIT/MAREA walks) even though sampling rate now is. A proper fix would mean harmonizing segment duration across datasets built from fundamentally different recording protocols, judged too large a redesign for this round, consistent with the same disclose-rather-than-fully-redesign decision already made for harmonic ratio's own frequency-resolution confound in round 17. Disclosed in `signal_utils.py` and Table 6's sample-entropy row.

**M11, confirmed already-correct in the manuscript, but stale in the notebook, enhanced disclosure**: the 17.5%-vs-0% Felius stroke/healthy stride-detection dropout was already correctly disclosed at P165/Section 4.2.2. The notebook's own "Placement synthesis" table (Part 2), however, was still citing a stale "44% dropout" figure computed on an earlier, incomplete 34-subject Felius pipeline (pre-dating the `Data_long` fix). Fixed the notebook to cite the current, confirmed 17.5% figure and clarified that this dropout affects foot signal-derived timing features specifically (stride-frequency detection), not foot accelerometer RMS, which needs no frequency detection, meaning it was never really evidence for a lower-back-vs-foot placement difference in the first place, the framing an earlier round's notebook text had used it for.

**M12, investigated and confirmed as an already-resolved non-issue, no new action taken**: the critic's concern that Felius's `Data_Stroke/` folder contains both `S001H`- and `S001P`-style participant IDs risking subject double-counting was already thoroughly investigated and disclosed in an earlier, pre-session round, `wiki/datasets/felius-dataset.md` already documents the 133-vs-107 stroke-participant-count discrepancy this naming pattern produces, and the reconciliation is already present in the manuscript at P165/Section 2.3.

**A second, previously-undiscovered instance of the same M2 bug found while fixing M2**: P165 restates the harmonic-ratio direction-agreement claim a second time later in the same paragraph, in the per-healthy-dataset individual-significance-check section ("Harmonic ratio separates the pooled stroke group from Camargo, DUO-GAIT, MAREA, and OxWalk alike in the same direction now"). Missed on the first read-through since the paragraph is long enough that the two instances are not adjacent, found only by grepping the fixed paragraph for "OxWalk" a second time after the first fix, to confirm no internal contradiction remained. Fixed to match the corrected framing.

**Code fixes**: `signal_utils.poincare_sd()` now requires `fs` (breaking change, all 4 call sites updated). `gaitmotion.py`'s docstring corrected. A residual small-n disclosure comment added to `signal_utils.sample_entropy()`.

**Notebook fixes**: cell 15 (Voisard participant-level ranking) variable assignment fixed. New Felius participant-level ranking cell added, previously entirely absent. `foot_accel_rms_mean` added to both placement maps. Cell 56 (placement synthesis table) rewritten with correct foot-beats-lower-back findings and the corrected 17.5% dropout figure. Cell 62 (nonlinear-feature direction disagreement) corrected, harmonic ratio is actually lower for the pathological group in both datasets now, a point of agreement, not disagreement, stale since before the round-16/17 Voisard concatenation fix. Cell 67 (SNR ranking interpretation) corrected to 3rd place. Cells 79/80 (pooled stride-time-CV discussion) corrected, a stale p = .24 left over from before the Felius `Data_long` fix was replaced with the current p = .91, and the OxWalk-exclusion finding added with a new reproducible verification cell. Cell 81 (Summary and next steps) RQ1 and RQ2 paragraphs rewritten to match all of the above. Full notebook re-executed twice this round, once after the code fixes and once after the final cell additions, zero errors both times.

**Manuscript fixes**: P154 (SNR ranking, rank 2/3 swap), P165 (harmonic-ratio numbers and direction disagreement, two separate instances, plus the stride-time-variability OxWalk-exclusion rewrite), P166, P213, P215, P216, each restating a subset of the same two findings, all checked and fixed individually rather than assuming a fix to one propagated. Table 6's Sample Entropy and Poincare SD1 rows updated with the M9/M10 disclosures. Every new sentence checked against this project's own comma-density (3-comma cap) and casual-connective rules, 7 sentences restructured, mostly split at the point the logical relationship changes, matching this project's established preference over deleting commas and running clauses together, and one mid-sentence "so" fixed to "and therefore." ## [2026-08-01] fix | Round 19 full-codebase pattern re-audit, three real live bugs found and fixed, a second recurring bug class identified

Direct response to explicit user feedback that prior rounds had been declaring checks "clean" without them actually being exhaustive, then finding real gaps only after a subagent or a later round caught them. Saved as a standing project memory ([[feedback_verify_before_declaring_clean]]) and applied here: every dataset module's own source code (`voisard.py`, `felius.py`, `duogait.py`, `oxwalk.py`, `marea.py`, `camargo.py`, `gaitmotion.py`, `cross_dataset.py`, `signal_utils.py`) was read in full end to end, not recalled from memory of what earlier rounds concluded, and every statistical test call in the whole notebook was enumerated by grep rather than sampled.

**Confirmed real and fixed — Voisard's SNR ranking (P154, Table 6), the largest finding**: `feature_snr()` was being called on `voisard_lit_with_age`, the raw trial-level table (488 trials, 122 participants, up to 29 trials for one person), even though this exact cell sits two cells away from the correctly participant-level age-adjustment fixed in round 16. Recomputing at the participant level leaves ranks 1-3 unchanged (foot RMS, lower-back RMS, sample entropy) but reshuffles 4-7: harmonic ratio moves from 7th place (F=81.6) to 4th (F=60.7), ahead of head RMS and both spatiotemporal timing features, directly contradicting the manuscript's "harmonic ratio ranked seventh... not lowest" claim. Fixed in notebook cells 65-67, P154, and the registry's SNR table.

**Confirmed real and fixed — Voisard's within-HS gender-confound check (P142, P206, P213), the most consequential reversal**: the same trial-level bug, in a different subsection of the same section. At the correct participant level, the raw comparison no longer shows "every feature significant at p<.001" — only the two accelerometer RMS features clear p<.05 (lb p=.015, he p=.025), and cadence/stride-time do not (p=.07-.19). More importantly, the age-adjusted comparison, which the manuscript reported as leaving "cadence and both stride-time measures... a genuine, age-independent gender effect on gait timing," in fact shows **nothing significant at all** once corrected (all p ≥ .08). This is a full reversal of a specific, three-times-repeated manuscript claim, not a refinement of it. The age-by-gender-within-HS finding itself survives (p=8.4e-4 at participant level) but was wildly overstated at the trial level (p=3.2e-15) by counting each participant's own identical, repeated age value once per trial. Fixed in notebook cells 19-23 and 81, and manuscript P142 (full rewrite), P206 (recommendation reframed around the false-positive this review's own analysis produced), P213 (one sentence reversed).

**Confirmed real and fixed — OxWalk's sex-by-cadence demographic check (P142)**: a different flavor of the same underlying problem. `oxwalk.build_feature_table()` returns two rows per participant (Hip_100Hz and Wrist_100Hz), and since OxWalk derives cadence from the same ground-truth step annotations regardless of placement, pooling both rows for a significance test literally duplicates each of the 39 participants' own cadence value rather than testing 78 genuinely independent observations. At the correct one-row-per-participant level (Hip only), cadence's sex difference is not significant, p=.070, not the p=.0095 the manuscript had reported as "nominally significant." DUO-GAIT's own equivalent finding (stride length, p=.028) is unaffected, since DUO-GAIT's own `build_feature_table()` already returns one row per subject per condition. Fixed in notebook cells 33-34 and 81, and manuscript P142.

**A second, distinct recurring bug class identified and documented, beyond round 18's "a fix changes a number, never re-verified downstream" pattern**: this round's three bugs share a different shape — a participant-level fix gets correctly applied to one analysis in a section, but a nearby analysis in the same section, built from the same underlying trial-level table, is never checked and silently stays at trial level. Documented in the registry's watchlist so future edits check every cell reading a raw `*_features` variable directly, not just the one a fix was originally requested for.

**Every other historical bug-pattern class re-verified clean via direct code reading, not re-derivation from a prior round's conclusion**: segment-concatenation confound (confirmed via full source read that only Voisard and OxWalk construct a continuity-sensitive feature's input by concatenating non-contiguous ranges — DUO-GAIT/MAREA/Camargo/Felius/GaitMotion all pass a single contiguous array), step/stride harmonic-ambiguity flag (confirmed consistently applied to every dataset/placement combination that needs it and deliberately absent from the ones that shouldn't have it, per each dataset's own already-documented justification), unit conversion (confirmed g/deg-per-s canonicalization is applied everywhere raw signal magnitude crosses a dataset boundary, and that scale-invariant features, sample entropy, harmonic ratio, are correctly exempted from needing it). No further live bugs found in these three categories.

Full notebook re-executed after all fixes, zero errors, numbers cross-checked against independent standalone script output before being written into the manuscript. Structural integrity confirmed unchanged (261 paragraphs, 7 tables, 4 inline shapes), zero semicolons. New manuscript prose checked against comma-density and casual-connective rules directly, zero violations found in the newly-written text.

**Confirmed real and the most significant finding this round — a genuine duplicated participant in Felius's own public data release, not a bug in this project's own code**: subject `S001P` has an identical trial_key present in both the `Data_Healthy` and `Data_Stroke` folders, with byte-identical feature values in each copy (confirmed by direct inspection of both file sets and both rows' extracted features). This person had been counted as both a stroke and a healthy participant throughout every Felius-derived statistic in the manuscript. ## [2026-08-01] review | External feedback on the manuscript independently re-verified — one real finding fixed (Abstract placement framing), one clarity-only fix (P216 wording), several claims found stale or inaccurate, one reliability concern about the feedback's own citations

The user forwarded a structured piece of external feedback (five numbered sections, specific "Cell N" citations, a recommended-revisions list) claiming several manuscript/notebook contradictions. Per this project's own standing instruction not to trust a review's characterization without independent verification, every claim was checked against the live document and the live notebook before acting on any of it, rather than applying the recommended revisions directly.

**Confirmed real and fixed — the Abstract (P3) led with lower-back-first framing despite foot placement being the numerically stronger finding in both real stroke datasets**: "lower back and trunk placement emerged as a consistent discriminator... an advantage that survives..." was stated first, with foot's stronger result folded into a subordinate "though" clause. This is the one point in the feedback that held up. The Discussion (P190) and Conclusion (P213/P214) already got this balance right in earlier rounds (P190: "Lower back placement's advantage over head placement is real... Its advantage over foot placement specifically is not... Lower back therefore remains a strong, deployment-relevant finding rather than this review's uniquely strongest placement evidence") — the Abstract had simply never received the same treatment. Rewritten to lead with foot's advantage, preserving every other factual claim (lower back surviving age-adjustment, holding direction in the second dataset, beating head placement, partial literature support).

**Confirmed legitimate as a clarity issue, though not an actual contradiction — P216's OxWalk/harmonic-disambiguation sentence**: factually correct (verified: it correctly describes two temporally distinct issues, the current OxWalk-driven stride-time-CV null and an earlier, already-superseded harmonic-disambiguation bug), but dense enough that a fast read could conflate the two as competing explanations for the same result. Rewritten with explicit "separate issue from an even earlier, already-corrected bug" framing and explicit temporal ordering.

**Checked and found NOT valid, stale, or already resolved — most of the feedback's other claims**:
- "Harmonic ratio still ranks 7th in residual text" — false. The only two "seventh" mentions in the current document are this project's own correction-disclosure sentence ("fourth... not seventh as earlier rounds reported") and an unrelated "the seventh [of Table 6's feature types]" count reference. The feedback appears to have pattern-matched the word "seventh" inside the correction sentence itself and read it as a live contradiction, rather than recognizing the "X, not Y as earlier reported" construction as the correction it is.

## [2026-08-01] sync | Discussion (P190) expanded to explain plausible mechanisms for every placement with a real effect, not trunk alone

Direct follow-up to explicit user instruction: the mechanism paragraph in P190 previously worked through only trunk's own candidate explanation (postural/balance compensation, ruled against by the observed direction, versus a slower-walking-speed alternative) even though foot shows the larger effect size of the two. Rewritten to cover all three RMS placements this review's own re-mining actually measured, plus Hsu et al.'s (2018) literature-only shank result, while keeping every claim consistent with the "list findings, don't rank them" reframing applied earlier the same day.

**Shared direction, one explanation for all three**: confirmed directly against the feature tables that stroke participants show lower RMS than healthy participants at all three placements, not just trunk (Voisard age-adjusted r: lower back 0.57, head 0.31, foot 0.72, all with the same sign under this review's own convention). A slower walking speed producing smaller acceleration throughout the body fits all three at once and is stated as the more parsimonious explanation for the shared *direction*.

**Different magnitudes, three candidate reasons added**: (1) foot's larger effect size is attributed to proximity — ankle dorsiflexion loss, reduced push-off, and foot drop are primarily distal impairments, so a foot-worn sensor sits directly on the segment producing the reduced acceleration, while trunk registers an attenuated, several-joints-removed version of the same slowdown; (2) head's smaller effect size is attributed to active vestibular head stabilization during locomotion, a real, independently verified mechanism (confirmed via a fresh WebSearch against real sources — Nature Scientific Reports, PMC — before writing this claim into the manuscript, per this project's own never-fabricate-a-claim standing rule, since this was new content this review had not previously stated) that would compress rather than eliminate the group difference at the head specifically; (3) Hsu et al.'s (2018) own shank-leading literature result is noted as consistent with the same proximity logic, explicitly caveated that this review's own re-mining includes no shank channel and cannot test that specific claim directly.

Every explanation is stated as a candidate, explicitly unconfirmed, matching the existing paragraph's own established epistemic style (gait speed itself was never measured directly or tested as a mediator) rather than asserted as settled fact. The pre-existing casual "so" in this paragraph's own closing sentence was fixed to a plain two-sentence split while already being edited for the new content. No other section required a corresponding update: a full-document grep for "mechanism" and "gait speed" found no other paragraph making a claim this expansion could contradict.

Structural integrity confirmed unchanged (261 paragraphs, 7 tables, 4 inline shapes), zero semicolons. New sentences checked against comma-density and casual-connective rules — all within cap once genuine lists and appositive pairs are correctly excluded.
