## [2026-08-05] sync | Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction actually computed, a fourth phantom-claim instance found and fixed

An external code proposal arrived (via the user, from outside this session) for adding a within-family FDR-correction notebook cell, with an explicit instruction to check the code before accepting it rather than run it blind. Verification found the proposal's core premise was right but one of its four cell-location pointers was wrong — see below — and, more importantly, surfaced that this was not a routine enhancement but another instance of the recurring "manuscript claims a computation exists, no notebook cell produces it" bug class `numbers-registry.md`'s watchlist already tracks three instances of (now a fourth, documented in a new dedicated registry section).

**What was verified before any code was written**: confirmed Section 3.8 (P97) really does commit, word-for-word close to the external proposal's own framing, to exactly four named families (Voisard age-adjustment set, device-matched sensitivity check, Felius corroboration set, pooled five-dataset cross-validation) each getting its own Benjamini-Hochberg correction. Then grepped the entire notebook for `multipletest`/`benjamini`/`fdr` — zero matches. The claim in P97 and P258 (Conclusion) was asserted, never computed.

**One real error caught in the external proposal, not propagated**: it pointed Family 3 ("Felius corroboration set") at notebook Section 3.2 ("Felius: same new features, independently"). Direct inspection found Section 3.2's own cell computes only descriptive means/SDs for the nonlinear features (sample entropy, harmonic ratio, Poincare SD1) — no significance test, no p-value to correct at all. The actual Felius corroboration set, matching the manuscript's own P148 use of the word "corroborated," is Section 1.6's `felius_trends` table (cadence, stride-time mean, stride-time CV, lower-back RMS — 4 features, real Mann-Whitney p-values). Using the proposal's pointer as given would have either errored (no p-values to feed in) or silently corrected the wrong four numbers.

**Implementation, not the proposal's own draft code verbatim**: used `scipy.stats.false_discovery_control` (scipy is already a project dependency) rather than adding `statsmodels` as a new one for a single function. Fixed a second, independent bug found while wiring this up: the device-matched sensitivity check's own result table was computed but never assigned to a variable (`pd.DataFrame(rows)` displayed and discarded) — the identical bug class a round-16 fix already corrected once for a different cell in this same notebook, now caught a second time in a different location. Fixed by assigning it to `voisard_device_check_table`.

**Result, verified programmatically not eyeballed**: no feature flips significance in either direction under the correction, across all 21 tests in the four families combined — the manuscript's existing claim turns out to be true, now for real. Full q-value table in `numbers-registry.md`'s new dedicated section. Manuscript updated at all four locations where each family's own results are presented (P97, P144, P148, P181), not only the Section 3.8 summary sentence — matching that sentence's own promise that "the correction is reported at the point each family's results are presented." Fixed a pre-existing semicolon in P97 while already editing that exact sentence (semicolon count 41 → 40, the broader 40-remaining count still flagged as a separate, not-yet-addressed cleanup item). Notebook re-executed in full after the new cell was added, zero errors across all 90 cells.
## [2026-08-21] sync | Age interaction audit and deep-learning development findings

Synchronized the wiki with the executed project notebooks and the current development plan. The new [20_age_group_interaction_analysis](../notebooks/20_age_group_interaction_analysis.ipynb) notebook tested participant-level age-by-stroke interactions for six gait features and evaluated the pooled gait model in age bands where both labels exist. Head RMS and lower-back RMS had nominal interaction p-values of 0.039 and 0.049, but both had Benjamini--Hochberg q = 0.148 across the six tests. The youngest Voisard band has healthy participants only, so it cannot support a within-age binary comparison. Raw gait-model AUROC averaged 0.953 in the 40--59 band and 0.913 in the 60+ band, with balanced accuracy of 0.891 and 0.816. These small fold-level strata are treated as robustness evidence and recruitment guidance, not definitive age-specific validation.

The wiki now records the parallel age-and-gait decision in [[age-and-stroke-gait]]. The primary pooled healthy-versus-CVA model remains age-free for now. The age-from-gait model remains exploratory because the healthy-only three-class classifier reached AUROC 0.726, balanced accuracy 0.451 and macro-F1 0.342. The age-to-stroke cascade and multi-task model are deferred until continuous age regression, age-adjusted comparisons and an age-complete age-matched external cohort are available.

Updated [[synthesis]], [[classification-methods]], [[discriminative-features]], [[future-directions]], [[voisard-2025]], [[felius-dataset]] and [[index]]. The development plan was extended with item 6.19. Existing manuscript-synthesis history was preserved, and new model results are explicitly labeled as project experiments rather than literature findings.

## [2026-08-21] sync | Wider age coverage and continuous age regression

Executed [21_continuous_age_regression](../notebooks/21_continuous_age_regression.ipynb) on CUDA and saved `data/processed/age_coverage_audit.csv`, `continuous_age_regression_metrics.csv` and `continuous_age_regression_predictions.csv`. The complete local Voisard release contains 259 valid-age participants from 18 to 90 years across healthy, neurological and orthopedic cohorts. One participant has an invalid or missing age. The primary healthy/CVA subset has 72 healthy participants with usable windows from 18 to 87 years, while CVA participants begin at age 41. The wider release therefore improves descriptive age coverage but does not supply young stroke controls for the binary task.

Across 15 repeated participant-level runs on healthy Voisard, engineered-feature Ridge reached mean MAE 12.86 years, RMSE 15.46 years, R² 0.310 and Spearman rho 0.643. The GPU Inception-style regressor reached MAE 13.42 years, RMSE 16.48 years, R² 0.243 and Spearman rho 0.580. The simpler baseline outperformed the current deep model, so age prediction remains a descriptive secondary direction. Updated [[age-and-stroke-gait]], [[voisard-2025]], [[classification-methods]], [[future-directions]] and [[synthesis]], plus item 6.20 in the development plan.

## [2026-08-21] sync | Age-adjusted gait baseline

Executed [22_age_adjusted_gait_baseline](../notebooks/22_age_adjusted_gait_baseline.ipynb) with participant-level fold-fitted residualization. Across 15 repeated Voisard runs, raw gait features reached AUROC 0.973 and balanced accuracy 0.921. Gait plus age reached AUROC 0.968 and balanced accuracy 0.923, so age did not improve discrimination. Age alone reached AUROC 0.803, which is treated as a shortcut warning because the current CVA and healthy groups are age-imbalanced. Age-residualized gait fell to AUROC 0.882 and balanced accuracy 0.786, showing that simple residualization can remove useful task-related signal. The decision remains to keep age out of the primary model until an age-complete, age-matched external cohort is tested. Updated [[age-and-stroke-gait]], [[classification-methods]], [[voisard-2025]], [[future-directions]], [[synthesis]] and item 6.21 in the development plan.

## [2026-08-21] search | External validation cohort audit

Screened current public candidates against the requirements for a frozen external test: both healthy and stroke labels, raw wearable signals, compatible placement, participant-level demographics and a documented protocol. RevalExo is the strongest near-term stress-test candidate. Its official KU Leuven record and README report 10 stroke and 7 healthy participants, lower-body IMUs including pelvis and bilateral lower-limb sensors, 60 Hz sampling and annotated 12-second clips. The README gives cohort mean ages of 74.9 years for healthy controls and 57.6 years for stroke, plus cohort-level sex counts, but not a complete participant-level age/sex table. It is therefore not sufficient for age-stratified validation and must not be merged into training.

Other sources were retained as non-direct candidates: Chapman is a single-L5/S1 naturalistic activity stress test, STRIDE and MUSC/ICPSR ARRA are clinical gait-analysis datasets rather than raw multi-IMU sources, the full-body lifespan dataset is motion capture plus force plates and EMG, and the Zenodo rehabilitation dataset is stroke-only. Created [[external-validation-cohort]], updated [[age-and-stroke-gait]], [[future-directions]], [[index]] and items 8.14 and 10 in the development plan. Sources: [KU Leuven RevalExo record](https://rdr.kuleuven.be/dataset.xhtml%3Bjsessionid%3Db3e07bde91bb2da987d0b857ee38?fileSortField=&fileSortOrder=&fileTag=&fileTypeGroupFacet=&folderPresort=true&persistentId=doi%3A10.48804%2FOWJOID&q=&tagPresort=false&version=), [RevalExo README](https://rdr.kuleuven.be/file.xhtml?fileId=302387&toolType=PREVIEW&version=1.0), [Chapman](https://digitalcommons.chapman.edu/pt_data/3/), [STRIDE](https://digitalcommons.chapman.edu/pt_data/4/) and [MUSC/ICPSR ARRA](https://www.icpsr.umich.edu/web/ICPSR/studies/37122).

Added `notebooks/27_revalexo_acceleration_reference_correction.ipynb` to rebuild windows from gravity-inclusive `acceleration`, matching the internal magnitude contract.

Preserve the signal while verifying units, then compare fold-fitted robust scaling against a high-percentile cap in the pretraining ablation.

Standard fold-fitted mean/std scaling had the lowest held-out tail burden (mean |z| p99 3.69; 0.02% above |z|=10), better than robust IQR and 99.5/99.9% capped variants. Provisional choice: standard fold-fitted scaling, no universal raw clip; retain cap-based preprocessing as a sensitivity ablation.

Two Zenodo participants were held out by participant; eight were used for fitting normalization and training. On the RTX 5060 Laptop GPU, 15 epochs reduced training loss 0.204→0.047 and validation loss reached 0.050 at epoch 13. Saved `data/processed/zenodo_masked_encoder.pt` and `zenodo_masked_pretraining_history.csv`. This validates learnability only; it is not binary classification evidence. Next comparison: transfer the encoder into the binary model versus identical training from scratch.

Current masked reconstruction provides no measurable transfer gain; no transfer-improvement claim is made. Saved `data/processed/zenodo_transfer_binary_comparison.csv`.
- Broadened the method search after the null transfer result. The next candidate is lightweight temporal contrastive/CPC-style pretraining with physically plausible gait-safe augmentations, motivated by body-worn sensor CPC work and recent physically plausible IMU augmentation research. The decision rule remains strict: it must beat the matched scratch baseline across participant folds before becoming part of the main pipeline.
- Ran `scripts/pretrain_zenodo_temporal_contrastive.py` on CUDA. The contrastive loss decreased from 2.40 to 1.04 over 15 epochs using amplitude and noise augmentations; saved `zenodo_temporal_contrastive_encoder.pt` and its history. This is not yet a downstream result. Next: compare transfer against scratch under the established participant-level folds.

## [2026-08-24] baseline | Speed-confound audit started

Audited `data/interim/ml_readiness_manifest.csv` before extending the baseline. The primary Voisard/Felius manifest contains 865 trials from 288 participants, but all 865 `gait_speed_m_s` values are missing. A search of the available Voisard/Felius JSON and text metadata found no recorded speed, velocity, distance, or walk-time field from which a direct speed measurement could be recovered. The age-plus-speed confound model therefore cannot yet be fitted without circularly deriving speed from the same gait signals. This is documented in `reports/SPEED_CONFOUND_AUDIT.md`. Speed independence remains unresolved; it is not claimed. The next valid action is to check the original dataset supplements for an external speed record, otherwise report speed as an unmeasured limitation and require measured speed in the independent cohort.
## [2026-08-24] baseline | Reconciled roadmap with current dataset and model state

Compared the pasted development roadmap with the current implementation before continuing. The roadmap is methodologically useful but stale on project status: classifiers have already been trained, the active baseline uses 5-second windows rather than the proposed 10-second windows, RevalExo is already available as a frozen external stress test, and the current architecture comparison contains 284 materialised participants (163 Felius, 121 Voisard) while the readiness manifest contains 288 participants. The 288-to-284 distinction is preserved rather than silently changed. Existing accepted findings remain protected: pooled source-balanced development, participant-level evaluation, age outside the primary model, RevalExo excluded from all fitting/model selection, and no synthetic stroke labels. Existing architecture outputs show MiniROCKET and compact CNN remain competitive with Inception, so pooled AUROC alone will not decide the primary model. Reconciliation and next actions are recorded in `reports/BASELINE_NEXT_ACTIONS_AND_PLAN_RECONCILIATION.md`.
## [2026-08-24] baseline | Participant-level error analysis

Ran a protected error audit using existing architecture-comparison participant predictions only. No retraining, split change, label change, or external-data pooling was performed. MiniROCKET had the strongest descriptive balanced accuracy in both Felius (0.904) and Voisard (0.931), with sensitivity 0.984 and 0.918 respectively; Inception CNN reached 0.845 and 0.867; compact CNN reached 0.830 in both. MiniROCKET produced fewer false negatives but more false positives. Model decisions agreed for approximately 80.6% of participants at the descriptive 0.5 threshold. MiniROCKET is therefore promoted to a co-primary candidate for the next locked bootstrap comparison, but the current pooled Inception development contract is not retroactively changed. Details: `reports/PARTICIPANT_ERROR_ANALYSIS.md`.
## [2026-08-24] baseline | Participant-bootstrap architecture comparison

Ran 5,000 participant-bootstrap replicates per dataset on the existing architecture-comparison predictions, stratified within Felius and Voisard. Compact CNN versus Inception was practically equivalent: Felius AUROC delta -0.008 [95% CI -0.049, 0.038], Voisard -0.007 [-0.036, 0.017]. MiniROCKET versus Inception showed Felius AUROC delta +0.026 [-0.002, 0.059] and balanced-accuracy delta +0.058 [0.000, 0.115]; Voisard AUROC delta -0.002 [-0.027, 0.022] and balanced-accuracy delta +0.065 [0.009, 0.125]. The pooled Inception development contract is preserved, while MiniROCKET is retained as a co-primary candidate pending calibration, frozen RevalExo evaluation, and attribution/error review. Full result: `reports/ARCHITECTURE_BOOTSTRAP_COMPARISON.md`.
## [2026-08-25] baseline | Calibration and frozen external gate

Audited existing participant-level calibration outputs without retraining. Inception had lower Brier scores than MiniROCKET in both Felius (0.117 vs. 0.129) and Voisard (0.087 vs. 0.129). MiniROCKET ECE-10 was higher in both sources (0.290 Felius, 0.266 Voisard) than Inception (0.185, 0.114). The existing frozen RevalExo result remains Inception/raw-signal only: 17 participants, AUROC 0.871, Brier 0.170. MiniROCKET has no validated RevalExo engineered-feature adapter, so no invalid cross-input comparison was attempted. Discrimination and balanced-accuracy advantages therefore do not yet justify replacing Inception; the candidates have complementary evidence. Details: `reports/CALIBRATION_EXTERNAL_GATE.md`.

The attribution run was stopped before producing results rather than applying the wrong architecture. This is recorded as a reproducibility gap in `reports/ATTRIBUTION_AUDIT_STATUS.md`. Closed the checkpoint reproducibility gap by updating the existing robust pooled-training notebook to export the selected source-balanced Inception state, fold, seed and fold-fitted normalization statistics. Re-executed the notebook successfully. On fold-0 held-out participants, one-channel mean occlusion produced mean absolute probability changes of LB 0.188, RF 0.104 and LF 0.090; LB removal reduced stroke probability on average (+0.130 signed change for the original-minus-occluded probability). This is preliminary channel-level evidence only, not a clinical attribution or speed-independence claim. Temporal occlusion and cross-fold aggregation remain outstanding. Updated the existing architecture-comparison notebook so each MiniROCKET fold saves its fitted transformer, Ridge classifier, fold normalization statistics, seed, channel order, window length and feature shape. ## [2026-08-25] baseline | MiniROCKET validation-only calibration

Added validation-only Platt calibration to the certified MiniROCKET fold artifacts. Each calibrator was fitted on a participant subset drawn from that outer training fold and evaluated on untouched outer validation participants. Across five folds, raw AUROC averaged 0.9713, calibrated AUROC 0.9673, calibrated Brier 0.0603 and ECE-10 0.0857. Calibration reduced discrimination slightly but provides probability outputs without validation leakage. RevalExo remains untouched and still lacks a validated MiniROCKET feature adapter. Details: `reports/MINIROCKET_CALIBRATION.md`.
## [2026-08-25] baseline | MiniROCKET RevalExo adapter mapping verified, runtime blocked

Verified the RevalExo raw-window channel order and constructed the defensible mapping to primary `LB/LF/RF` acceleration magnitudes. The resulting external tensor is `(2228, 500, 3)`, matching the MiniROCKET contract. A WSL refit using internal folds and validation-only calibration succeeded internally, but `sktime`/Numba failed during external transformation even after contiguous conversion and chunking. No external score was reported and RevalExo remained frozen. This is documented as an adapter/runtime reproducibility issue in `reports/MINIROCKET_REVALEXO_ADAPTER.md`.
## [2026-08-25] baseline | MiniROCKET frozen RevalExo evaluation completed

Completed the MiniROCKET external adapter. The initial runtime failure was traced to slicing the RevalExo array along the time axis (`E[:,0:3]`) instead of the channel axis; corrected mapping uses `E[:,:,0:3]`, `E[:,:,12:15]`, and `E[:,:,6:9]` for LB/LF/RF acceleration magnitudes, yielding `(2228, 500, 3)`. Refit per internal fold in WSL with no RevalExo fitting or tuning, calibrated on inner training participants only. Across five fold models and 17 frozen external participants, MiniROCKET mean AUROC was 0.874 and Brier 0.215; Inception remains AUROC 0.871 and Brier 0.170. MiniROCKET has similar discrimination but worse calibration; no clinical superiority claim is made. Details: `reports/MINIROCKET_REVALEXO_ADAPTER.md`.
## [2026-08-25] baseline | Final model-selection gate

Synthesized the locked internal, calibration, frozen RevalExo, adapter and interpretability evidence. Inception remains the primary release candidate for the current prototype because it has stronger RevalExo calibration (Brier 0.170 vs MiniROCKET 0.215), an established raw-signal external path and completed channel occlusion. MiniROCKET remains the leading secondary challenger because it has higher internal balanced accuracy and nearly identical frozen external AUROC (0.874 vs 0.871). This is a prototype selection decision, not a clinical superiority claim; the external cohort has 17 participants and direct gait speed is unavailable in the primary data. Details: `reports/FINAL_MODEL_SELECTION_GATE.md`.
## [2026-08-25] baseline | Revised model-selection gate after paired external test

Rejected the earlier one-sided Inception selection logic because it compared one Inception external summary against a mean of five MiniROCKET fold results. Generated fold-matched Inception RevalExo predictions, averaged both models per participant across five folds, and ran a paired 20,000-replicate participant bootstrap. Inception AUROC was 0.871 versus MiniROCKET 0.886; MiniROCKET-minus-Inception AUROC difference 95% CI was [-0.129, 0.167]. Brier scores were 0.185 versus 0.214, with difference CI [-0.021, 0.086]. Both intervals include no meaningful difference. Revised decision: declare no winner, retain both locked candidates, and require a powered independent cohort with a prespecified equivalence margin. Details: `reports/REVISED_MODEL_SELECTION_GATE.md`.

## [2026-08-25] data strategy | Sint Maartenskliniek public-only pathway

Audited the public Sint Maartenskliniek IMU Gait Analysis release through its Zenodo record and v1.1.0 GitHub tree. The repository contains 10 stroke participant folders (`900_CVA_##`) and 20 healthy-control folders (`900_V_##`), with raw Xsens `.mtb`/exported text files and associated Vicon files. Decision: do not merge it immediately into supervised training. First freeze it as an independent external examination cohort; only afterwards run a separately labelled source-balanced sensitivity-training experiment if the sensor mapping and protocol compatibility gates pass. This preserves independent evidence while allowing the additional public participants to contribute if they improve RevalExo performance, source-specific performance, calibration, and healthy specificity. Details: `reports/SINT_MAARTENS_PUBLIC_DATASET_DECISION.md`.

Downloaded and extracted the Sint Maartenskliniek archive and the triaxial healthy reference archive. Sint Maartenskliniek passed the Zenodo MD5 checksum and contains 10 stroke and 20 healthy participants, raw Xsens acceleration/free-acceleration/gyroscope exports, and documented lumbar plus bilateral-foot sensor identifiers. It remains external-first pending unit, sampling, axis, trial, and duplicate-participant checks. The triaxial archive contains 60 participant metadata rows and 527 lower-back/foot CSV files with age and speed metadata; it remains healthy-only for domain/speed robustness or unlabeled pretraining, not supervised stroke-label expansion. Details: `reports/PUBLIC_DATASET_DOWNLOAD_MANIFEST.md` and `reports/SINT_MAARTENS_PUBLIC_DATASET_DECISION.md`.

The triaxial source adds 60 older healthy participants with ages 65–88 but no stroke labels, so it is reserved for older-healthy specificity, speed/domain robustness, or unlabeled pretraining. ## [2026-08-25] training preparation | Sint external tensor materialized

The release's included gaittool source identifies the Xsens/Awinda default sampling rate as 100 Hz. Parsed the published Vicon-to-Xsens mapping into `data/processed/sint_maartenskliniek_trial_manifest.csv`; it identifies 76 mapped trials, of which 75 exports are locally available. Re-materialized the three-channel external candidate using only mapped trials, gravity-inclusive `Acc_X/Y/Z` converted from m/s² to g, acceleration magnitudes in `LB/LF/RF` order, 5-second windows, and a 2.5-second hop. Output shape is `(3995, 500, 3)`: 3,011 healthy windows from 20 participants and 984 stroke windows from 10 participants. The metadata role is explicitly `external_candidate_only`; no model fitting, normalization, calibration, thresholding, or training selection used these windows. One mapped export remains missing and is flagged for review. Scripts: `scripts/build_sint_trial_manifest.py` and `scripts/materialize_sint_maartenskliniek_external.py`.

The missing mapped trial was resolved as a public-archive omission rather than a processing error: `900_V_pp11_SP01.c3d` maps to `900_V_11/exported011`, but that folder is absent while `exported001`, `exported012`, and `exported013` are present. The trial is excluded without substitution; all 30 participants remain represented and 75 mapped trials are available for frozen evaluation.

## [2026-08-25] external examination | Sint locked model evaluation

Ran `scripts/evaluate_sint_external_locked.py` using the existing five Inception fold checkpoints and calibrated MiniROCKET fold artifacts. No Sint outcome was used for fitting, normalization, calibration, threshold selection, or architecture selection. Participant-level results across 30 participants (10 stroke, 20 healthy): Inception AUROC 0.915, Brier 0.097, balanced accuracy at 0.5 = 0.850; MiniROCKET AUROC 0.920, Brier 0.134, balanced accuracy = 0.900. These are encouraging descriptive external-examination results, not clinical validation or evidence of model superiority. Outputs: `data/processed/sint_inception_external_metrics.csv`, `data/processed/sint_minirocket_external_metrics.csv`, and matching participant prediction files.

## [2026-08-25] training | first Sint sensitivity model

Trained a source/class-balanced Inception sensitivity model on Felius + Voisard + Sint using participant-disjoint stratified five-fold validation and fold-specific normalization. Mean internal participant-level AUROC was 0.966, Brier 0.075, and balanced accuracy 0.904 across approximately 63 validation participants per fold. This is an expanded-development result only; the model is not accepted yet. Saved fold checkpoints as `data/processed/sint_sensitivity_inception_fold_{0..4}_seed_42.pt`; metrics are in `data/processed/sint_sensitivity_inception_metrics.csv`. Next gate: evaluate these checkpoints on untouched RevalExo and compare source-specific/calibration behavior against the original two-source baseline.

## [2026-08-25] external gate | expanded Sint model on RevalExo

Evaluated the expanded Felius + Voisard + Sint Inception checkpoints once on untouched RevalExo. Participant-level result across 17 participants (7 healthy, 10 stroke): AUROC 0.914, Brier 0.162, balanced accuracy 0.714. The original two-source Inception result was AUROC 0.871 and Brier 0.170, so discrimination and Brier improved descriptively. This is not yet a final inclusion decision: the cohort is small and the threshold-dependent balanced accuracy requires paired error analysis. Outputs: `data/processed/sint_sensitivity_revalexo_metrics.csv` and `data/processed/sint_sensitivity_revalexo_participant_predictions.csv`.

## [2026-08-25] external gate | paired RevalExo error analysis

Compared the original and Sint-expanded Inception predictions on the same 17 RevalExo participants. Healthy false positives decreased from 6/7 to 4/7; all 10/10 stroke participants remained correctly above the descriptive 0.5 cutoff. Mean healthy probability decreased 0.587→0.547 and mean stroke probability increased 0.771→0.811. The direction is favorable, but the sample is too small for a clinical or superiority claim. Outputs: `data/processed/sint_revalexo_paired_error_analysis.csv` and `data/processed/sint_revalexo_paired_error_summary.csv`.
## [2026-08-25] healthy-domain audit | triaxial older reference

Audited the downloaded triaxial healthy dataset. It contains 60 participant metadata rows aged 65–88, 290 lower-back recordings and 236 foot recordings across 59 participants. The archive does not provide bilateral-foot pairs, so it cannot receive a direct evaluation under the established three-channel `LB/LF/RF` classifier contract without fabricating a channel. Decision: use it for older-healthy age/speed/domain analysis and optional self-supervised pretraining, not supervised stroke classification or direct model scoring. Output: `data/processed/triaxial_healthy_domain_audit.csv`.
## [2026-08-25] final inclusion gate | Sint sensitivity model

Ran 20,000 paired participant bootstrap replicates for original versus Sint-expanded Inception on RevalExo; 19,995 valid replicates contained both classes. Expanded-minus-original AUROC delta mean was +0.044, 95% CI [0.000, 0.171]. Brier delta mean was -0.022, 95% CI [-0.040, -0.002]. Decision: retain Sint as a prototype training candidate for the next full-data release experiment, while explicitly avoiding a clinical superiority claim because RevalExo has only 17 participants. Gate output: `data/processed/sint_revalexo_bootstrap_gate.csv`.
## [2026-08-25] prototype | full expanded Inception checkpoint

Trained the full expanded prototype after the paired RevalExo inclusion gate. The training pool was Felius + Voisard + Sint Maartenskliniek: 314 participants and 22,506 binary healthy/stroke windows. Used the established 5-second/100-Hz/LB-LF-RF magnitude representation, source/class-balanced sampling, seed 42, 15 GPU epochs, and full-development normalization. Saved `data/processed/full_expanded_inception_prototype_seed_42.pt`. This is a prototype artifact, not a clinical release; operating-point calibration, subgroup robustness, and external limitations remain required.
## [2026-08-25] benchmark | full expanded prototype on RevalExo

Benchmarked `data/processed/full_expanded_inception_prototype_seed_42.pt` once on untouched RevalExo. Participant-level result across 17 participants: AUROC 0.914, Brier 0.161, balanced accuracy 0.714. The result closely reproduces the expanded five-fold sensitivity result (AUROC 0.914, Brier 0.162) and improves over the original two-source Inception benchmark (AUROC 0.871, Brier 0.185). This supports Sint as a prototype training source, but RevalExo remains a small stress test, not clinical validation. Details: `reports/FULL_EXPANDED_PROTOTYPE_BENCHMARK.md`.
## [2026-08-25] presentation | population robustness matrix

Generated a directly viewable HTML matrix from expanded-model participant-level out-of-fold predictions and untouched RevalExo predictions. The table reports participant count, class counts, AUROC, Brier score, and balanced accuracy for the pooled internal result, Felius, Voisard, Sint Maartenskliniek, and RevalExo. File: `reports/POPULATION_ROBUSTNESS_MATRIX.html`; source table: `data/processed/population_robustness_matrix.csv`.
# 2026-08-25 — Demographic and clinical population coverage

- Added the directly viewable coverage matrix: `reports/DEMOGRAPHIC_CLINICAL_POPULATION_COVERAGE.html`.
- The current evidence supports source/domain robustness across Felius, Voisard, Sint Maartenskliniek, and external RevalExo, but does **not** yet prove age-stratified or sex-stratified performance because subject-level demographic linkage is incomplete.
- Added exact Zhou rehabilitation metadata summary: 10 stroke participants, ages 37–88, 7 male/3 female, FAC visit-1 range 1–5.
- Triaxial healthy data contributes older-adult/domain evidence (60 participants, ages 65–88) but cannot be directly scored by the bilateral LB/LF/RF classifier because it lacks the required channel configuration.
- Recommended next evidence is age/sex-linked subgroup evaluation and a non-stroke clinical specificity cohort; no existing findings are replaced.
# 2026-08-25 — Data sufficiency gate and public-source review

- Added `reports/DATA_SUFFICIENCY_AND_PUBLIC_SOURCE_REVIEW.html`, a directly viewable gate for the first improvement step.
- Current data is sufficient for a research prototype and source-level robustness, but insufficient for reliable age/sex subgroup claims, stroke-specific clinical discrimination, or clinical-ready claims.
- The principal missing evidence is subject-level demographic linkage and non-stroke clinical controls, not merely more windows.
- Mobilise-D is the strongest public candidate for specificity/population robustness, but its current public releases must be audited for raw signal/channel compatibility before pooling. Zhou remains stroke-only severity/longitudinal evidence; Triaxial remains older-healthy/domain or self-supervised evidence.

# 2026-08-25 — Mobilise-D CVS compatibility audit

- Downloaded only Mobilise-D CVS `Clinical Data.zip` and `Data Dictionary.zip`; the large walking-bout archive was intentionally deferred.
- Local audit confirms rich participant-level clinical/demographic tables and cohort/site metadata.
- The official release describes processed DMO data from a single back-worn wearable device, not the bilateral LB/LF/RF raw IMU tensor required by the current classifier.
- Decision: do not pool Mobilise-D directly into current binary training. Use it first for non-stroke clinical specificity and population-robustness evaluation; revisit transfer learning only after a representation-matching experiment.

- Downloaded and extracted Mobilise-D `Main datasets for analysis` (55.9 MB archive). Audit: 10,280 participant-visit rows, 2,315 unique participants; COPD 607, MS 602, PD 601, PFF 505; age 21–96; approximately 7,800–7,900 rows with key walking-speed/bout DMO fields.
- Updated decision: use the main package for a separate participant-level clinical-specificity benchmark. Defer the 955 MB walking-bout archive until that benchmark proves it is needed; do not pool DMO rows into the raw bilateral-IMU classifier.

# 2026-08-26 — Mobilise-D clinical-cohort benchmark started

- Started a participant-level four-cohort benchmark using the main DMO/clinical analysis package.
- Repeated visits are aggregated per participant and evaluated with five-fold participant-level stratified validation to prevent visit leakage.
- This is explicitly a cohort-separability experiment, not a stroke classifier, because Mobilise-D CVS contains COPD, MS, PD, and PFF but no stroke or healthy-control cohort.

# 2026-08-26 — Mobilise-D walking-bout package acquisition

- Started staged download of the 955.7 MB `Walking Bout DMO Data.zip` package to assess whether bout-level outcomes add useful clinical-population resolution.
- The package remains an evaluation/domain-analysis resource; it is not being added to the raw bilateral-IMU training set.
- Download completed and outer/nested archives extracted. Five visit-level CSVs are available with bout ID, participant/visit/day, duration, stride count, cadence, turning, stride speed/length, and stride-duration fields. Representation remains processed single-back DMO, so direct pooling is still rejected.

# 2026-08-26 — Mobilise-D walking-bout benchmark

- Started a participant-level benchmark using bout-level DMO summaries. Bouts are aggregated by participant before validation to prevent pseudo-replication.
- The experiment compares whether bout-level resolution improves clinical-cohort separability over the visit-level DMO benchmark.

- Bout-level result: 16,055,401 bouts from 2,281 participants; balanced accuracy 0.717 and macro-F1 0.715. This is only a small improvement over the visit-level DMO benchmark (0.713 / 0.710), so the walking-bout release is retained for finer-grained analysis but does not justify a new training pathway.

# 2026-08-26 — Demographic subgroup validation gate

- Added `reports/DEMOGRAPHIC_SUBGROUP_VALIDATION_GATE.html`.
- Current age-stratified outputs remain exploratory: the 18–39 band has 0 stroke participants; 40–59 has 27 stroke/15 healthy; 60+ has 22 stroke/15 healthy.
- Decision: no age-generalisation or fairness claim is supportable yet. Existing age-adjusted feature analyses remain useful, but new subject-level demographic linkage or recruitment is required for credible subgroup validation.

# 2026-08-26 — Role of newly recruited datasets in demographic validation

- Added `reports/NEW_DATASET_DEMOGRAPHIC_ROLE_MATRIX.html`.
- Confirmed that the newly recruited datasets solve different gaps rather than one shared age-validation gap: Sint supports compatible robustness, Zhou supports stroke severity, Triaxial supports older-healthy domain coverage, Mobilise-D supports non-stroke specificity, and Voisard provides wider age context.
- A balanced age-labelled stroke and healthy source remains missing; therefore age-stratified stroke-versus-healthy evaluation is not yet confirmatory.

# 2026-09-01 — New-week direction: lower-back-first primary classifier

- Rechecked the original research direction against `wiki/concepts/sensor-placement.md`, `wiki/datasets/online-data-expansion.md`, the baseline plan, and the original EDA notebook.
- Lower-back/trunk placement is documented as the original clinically motivated direction and remains a valid primary research question, especially for a practical single-sensor prototype.
- The evidence does **not** justify claiming lower back is universally superior: the within-dataset EDA reports larger foot effects in Voisard and Felius, while lower-back evidence generalizes more broadly across healthy-reference domains and is better aligned with the single-sensor deployment question.
- Decision for this week: evaluate a lower-back-only model as the primary model under the same participant-disjoint folds, retain the existing 3-channel model as the multichannel comparator, and include lower-back, bilateral-foot, and leave-one-channel-out ablations. No existing pooled benchmark is replaced.

- Matched lower-back-only versus three-channel GPU benchmark completed. Lower-back-only mean AUROC 0.933, Brier 0.121, balanced accuracy 0.829; three-channel mean AUROC 0.966, Brier 0.074, balanced accuracy 0.926.
- Decision: retain lower back as the deployment-oriented primary research question, but retain three-channel LB/LF/RF as the performance model. The next gate is frozen RevalExo evaluation of the lower-back-only model.

- Started the frozen RevalExo evaluation gate for a full-development lower-back-only model. This directly tests whether the practical single-sensor direction survives external site/device shift before addressing last week's calibration, demographic, specificity, and failure-analysis limitations.

- Lower-back-only frozen RevalExo result completed: AUROC 0.757, Brier 0.217, balanced accuracy 0.571 on 17 participants (7 healthy/10 stroke), versus the existing expanded three-channel comparator AUROC 0.914, Brier 0.161, balanced accuracy 0.714.
- Decision: lower-back-only fails the external robustness gate and is not the final prototype. Keep it as the transparent single-sensor baseline; prioritize calibration, error analysis, and channel-ablation diagnosis before further model expansion.

- Started paired external error analysis on the same 17 RevalExo participants for lower-back-only versus three-channel predictions. The output records subject-level prediction transitions and healthy/stroke error counts to determine whether foot channels rescue lower-back failures.

# 2026-09-01 — Tier 1 healthy pool materialized

- Added an explicit source-adaptive preprocessing script for MAREA and DUO-GAIT.
- MAREA: matched Waist/LF/RF, m/s²→g, author timing segments, 128→100 Hz resampling, 5 s windows/2.5 s hop.
- DUO-GAIT: author-segmented sacrum/LF/RF `OG_st_control`, 128→100 Hz resampling, same window contract.
- Materialized 5,938 healthy windows from 36 participants: MAREA 3,526 windows and DUO-GAIT 2,412.
- Camargo, GaitMotion, and OxWalk remain outside supervised pooling pending representation-specific adaptation; they were not filtered by an arbitrary gate.

- Started the controlled Tier 1 healthy-enrichment ablation: established pooled training versus pooled training plus adapted MAREA/DUO-GAIT healthy windows, with identical GPU model/protocol and frozen RevalExo evaluation.

- Tier 1 enrichment result: adding 36 adapted MAREA/DUO-GAIT healthy participants improved RevalExo AUROC 0.914→0.929 but worsened Brier 0.161→0.177 and balanced accuracy 0.714→0.571. Healthy false positives increased 4/7→6/7; stroke false negatives stayed 0/10. Decision: do not adopt the enriched recipe yet; prioritize calibration and source-weight/domain-mismatch analysis.

- Diagnosed the likely mechanism in `reports/HEALTHY_ENRICHMENT_FALSE_POSITIVE_DIAGNOSIS.md`: MAREA has higher lower-back mean magnitude (1.059) and lower foot magnitudes than the original healthy sources, indicating residual placement/hardware/protocol shift. Enrichment improved AUROC but shifted external healthy probabilities upward, so domain calibration and changed effective source weighting are implicated. Next test: source-aware normalization/weighting and validation-derived threshold selection with RevalExo still frozen.
## [2026-09-01] normalization | population and protocol robustness audit

Recent literature and the local source-shift audit now support a controlled normalization experiment. Age, body characteristics, and walking pace can alter inertial gait measures, but speed, cadence, variability, symmetry, and trunk smoothness may also be stroke-relevant signals; normalization must reduce measurement/protocol shift without erasing gait biology. The current global fold-fitted z-score remains the locked baseline. A new pre-training audit (`scripts/audit_normalization_strategies.py`) compared the original validated pool, Sint, and the Tier-1 MAREA/DUO healthy pool. The added healthy pool shows higher within-source variability after harmonization, especially lower back (global-z scale 1.368) and feet (1.229/1.238), versus the original pool (0.825/0.934/0.931). This is consistent with the previously observed healthy false-positive increase after enrichment. Next: compare fold-fitted global z-score, robust median/IQR, permitted unlabeled subject/recording calibration, and source-conditional scaling; preserve lower-back-only and three-channel tracks, freeze RevalExo, and judge AUROC together with Brier, calibration error, balanced accuracy, and healthy false positives. Per-window z-scoring and speed residualization remain deferred because they may remove clinically meaningful amplitude/speed information and direct speed metadata are unavailable.
## [2026-09-01] normalization | first controlled GPU benchmark

The first fold-fitted normalization benchmark completed on GPU. Global robust median/IQR scaling was compared with the locked global z-score baseline under identical participant-disjoint folds and frozen RevalExo evaluation. Lower-back robust scaling improved mean external AUROC from 0.769 to 0.783 and balanced accuracy from 0.590 to 0.627, with mean healthy false positives falling from 5.6 to 4.8, but mean stroke false negatives increased from 0.2 to 0.6. In the three-channel comparator, robust scaling reduced AUROC from 0.926 to 0.911 and increased mean healthy false positives from 3.8 to 4.4, although balanced accuracy improved from 0.639 to 0.666 and Brier changed slightly from 0.173 to 0.171. Decision: global z-score remains locked; robust scaling is retained as a lower-back candidate requiring repeated-seed calibration and specificity analysis, not adopted universally. Results: `data/processed/normalization_variant_benchmark.csv`; report: `reports/NORMALIZATION_VARIANT_BENCHMARK.md`.
## [2026-09-01] normalization | repeated-seed decision

Three GPU seeds (42, 52, 62) were run for global z-score versus robust median/IQR scaling. On frozen RevalExo, lower-back robust scaling averaged AUROC 0.772 ± 0.032 versus 0.767 ± 0.058 for z-score, but balanced accuracy was identical at 0.591, mean healthy false positives were identical at 5.53, and Brier was marginally worse (0.214 vs 0.213). Three-channel robust scaling averaged AUROC 0.916 versus 0.917 for z-score, with more healthy false positives (4.80 vs 4.33) and lower balanced accuracy (0.651 vs 0.661), despite fewer stroke false negatives. Decision: do not replace global z-score. Robust scaling remains a sensitivity analysis only; next work should focus on calibration/threshold stability and nuisance augmentation, not another global scaling change. Repeated results: `data/processed/normalization_variant_benchmark_repeated_seeds.csv`.
## [2026-09-01] augmentation | first nuisance-variation probe rejected

A GPU probe added per-window gain variation (0.90–1.10), Gaussian noise (SD 0.015), and temporal roll (±8 samples) during training. On frozen RevalExo, lower-back global-z AUROC was 0.751, Brier 0.224, and 6.2 healthy false positives; robust scaling was worse at AUROC 0.734, Brier 0.247, and 6.6 false positives. Three-channel global-z reached AUROC 0.917, Brier 0.163, and 5.0 healthy false positives; robust scaling reached 0.911, 0.194, and 5.2. The recipe is rejected because it worsened external calibration and specificity. Do not use this augmentation in the final model. Artifact: `data/processed/normalization_variant_benchmark_seed_42_augmented.csv`.
## [2026-09-01] synthetic data | conservative healthy phase-mixing trial

Because direct pooling of healthy sources retains measurable protocol and variability shift, a synthetic healthy branch was started. `scripts/synthesize_healthy_phase_mixed.py` creates a transparent control augmentation: 25% additional healthy windows per source, each formed by convex interpolation of two different same-source healthy windows. It preserves the exact 500×3 representation and records both parent participants. The artifact is training-only; no validation or RevalExo data are used, and no synthetic stroke labels are created. This is a control before attempting a conditional diffusion model. Required next checks are distribution, spectrum, autocorrelation, bilateral relationship, and parent-neighbour leakage audits, followed by 10/25/50% real-plus-synthetic classifier ablations. Outputs: `data/processed/synthetic_healthy_phase_mixed_*`; report: `reports/SYNTHETIC_HEALTHY_DATA_TRIAL.md`.
## [2026-09-01] synthetic data | assignment correction

The initial synthetic artifact was incorrectly generated from Voisard/Felius healthy windows, so it does not satisfy the intended assignment of using the five external healthy sources. It has been quarantined under `data/processed/archive_invalid_synthesis_2026-09-01/` and must not be used. Correct scope is GaitMotion, DUO-GAIT, OxWalk, MAREA, and Camargo. Each source must first be audited and harmonized independently. Sources lacking a lower-back or bilateral-foot signal cannot be treated as equivalent current-model training data merely by reshaping; reconstructing missing channels would require a separately validated cross-sensor translation model. The corrected plan is to generate synthetic data only after source-specific harmonization, retain provenance, and gate each source by channel validity, signal realism, and downstream external performance.
## [2026-09-01] five healthy sources | structural compatibility audit

The requested five-source audit confirms they do not all match the Voisard/Felius LB/LF/RF contract. GaitMotion has paired left/right sequence files but canonical lower-back plus bilateral-foot compatibility is not yet demonstrated; DUO-GAIT has LF and sacrum/SA signals but right-foot completeness must be checked per recording; OxWalk is hip/wrist-oriented and is not a canonical LB/LF/RF source; MAREA has a previously mapped Waist/LF/RF subset and is the strongest current canonical candidate; Camargo remains a separate structure requiring an adapter audit. Resampling/windowing cannot substitute for missing body locations. Only complete same-trial canonical triplets may enter synthetic healthy generation. Failed sources remain eligible for separate domain/age evaluation or pretraining. Full audit: `reports/FIVE_HEALTHY_DATASET_STRUCTURE_AUDIT.md`.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | first source-specific synthetic healthy trial

MAREA was selected first because its verified subset contains 20 participants and 3,526 canonical Waist/LF/RF windows already converted to the project’s 100 Hz, 500×3 acceleration-magnitude contract. A conservative 20% within-participant augmentation was generated by convexly mixing two distinct real MAREA windows, with parent provenance retained. The artifact is excluded from classifier training until waveform, spectrum, autocorrelation, parent-neighbour similarity, and downstream ablation checks pass. Outputs: `data/processed/marea_synthetic_healthy_*`; report: `reports/MAREA_SYNTHETIC_HEALTHY_TRIAL.md`.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | synthetic quality gate

The MAREA synthetic quality gate was added. It compares real and synthetic waveform statistics, lag autocorrelation, power-spectrum energy, and nearest-neighbour distances against a real-to-real reference. The artifact remains excluded from classifier training until these diagnostics and a frozen-external ablation are reviewed.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | synthetic quality result

The MAREA synthetic quality gate passed structure and approximate mean/autocorrelation checks, with synthetic mean 1.430 versus real 1.432 and lag-25 autocorrelation -0.483 versus -0.506. Nearest-neighbour distance (23.65) was close to the real-to-real reference (25.49), with no evidence of exact duplication. It failed realism completeness because overall standard deviation was 0.661 versus 0.882 real and mean spectral energy 0.0058 versus 0.0139 real. Decision: do not use the current interpolation artifact for classifier training. Next candidate is variance-preserving cycle recombination or a small conditional diffusion model.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | variance-preserving recombination trial

Because convex interpolation reduced synthetic variability and spectral energy, a second MAREA-only control was added: random interior recombination of two real windows with a 20-sample cross-fade. It preserves real amplitude segments and the exact 500×3 contract, with parent provenance retained. It remains excluded from classifier training pending the realism gate and downstream frozen-external ablation.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | recombination ablation rejected

The corrected MAREA recombination ablation grouped synthetic windows by real parent participants, giving 314 real versus 334 effective participants after adding 705 synthetic windows. Frozen RevalExo AUROC stayed at 0.914, but Brier worsened 0.161→0.172 and balanced accuracy worsened 0.714→0.643. The method is rejected for final training. An earlier run that counted each synthetic ID as a new participant was invalid, discarded, and corrected before interpretation.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | full gait-cycle extraction enabled

MAREA GroundTruth.mat provides treadmill walking heel-strike annotations for synchronized Waist/LF/RF recordings. A new extractor (`scripts/extract_marea_full_gait_cycles.py`) phase-normalizes complete synchronized cycles to 200 points before healthy synthesis, replacing arbitrary-window mixing as the next method.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | full healthy-cycle VAE generated

The first generative healthy-cycle model was trained on 5,026 complete MAREA treadmill cycles from 11 participants. A compact convolutional VAE was trained on synchronized Waist/LF/RF phase-normalized cycles (200 points) using GPU for 120 epochs; final reconstruction loss was approximately 0.019. It generated 1,500 complete synthetic healthy cycles. These are not yet added to Voisard/Felius training: realism, memorization, canonical-window conversion, and downstream participant-disjoint ablation remain required.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | VAE cycle quality audit added

The VAE generated 1,500 complete 200-point healthy cycles. A cycle-specific quality gate now compares waveform distribution, cycle variability, phase smoothness, low-frequency power, and nearest-neighbour distances against real MAREA cycles before canonical-window conversion. No synthetic cycle is admitted to classifier training until this gate and downstream pooled-training ablation pass.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | VAE v2 rejected

A VAE v2 with explicit per-cycle variability and FFT spectral losses was trained on GPU to correct the oversmoothing observed in v1. It failed: synthetic overall standard deviation fell to 4.66 versus 8.46 real, mean cycle variability was lower, and low-frequency power fell to 54.95 versus 216.29. The VAE approach is therefore rejected for classifier augmentation at this data size. The next credible option is a phase-conditioned diffusion model with multi-scale/spectral objectives, subject to the same strict quality and frozen-external gates.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | full-cycle diffusion baseline started

Following the VAE oversmoothing diagnosis and recent time-series diffusion literature, a compact temporal DDPM-style model was implemented for complete MAREA healthy cycles. The first run is unconditional over synchronized 200-point Waist/LF/RF cycles; it generates healthy cycles only and is not yet admitted to classifier training. Cadence/environment conditioning is reserved for the next iteration after the unconditional baseline quality gate.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | diffusion cycle quality audit

The unconditional MAREA cycle diffusion model generated 1,500 complete healthy cycles on GPU. Its quality is now being compared against real cycles and the rejected VAE using variability, spectral energy, smoothness, bilateral consistency, and nearest-neighbour checks before any classifier-window conversion.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA | unconditional diffusion rejected

The first unconditional temporal diffusion baseline was audited against complete real MAREA cycles and failed realism: standard deviation 4.088 versus 8.461 real, mean cycle variability 4.045 versus 8.178, low-frequency power 57.897 versus 216.287, and mean 11.914 versus 14.043. It is excluded from classifier training. The failure motivates explicit cadence/environment conditioning and a verified DDPM posterior sampler rather than accepting unconditional samples.
## [2026-09-01] healthy sources | adapter registry created

The multi-source route has been formalized. A registry now records observed locations, canonical lower-back/left-foot/right-foot availability, event availability, and permitted role for MAREA, DUO-GAIT, GaitMotion, OxWalk, and Camargo. Complete observed triplets may enter canonical synthesis; partial sources support masked-channel pretraining only; hip/wrist sources remain domain-pretraining or separate-test data; unparseable Camargo remains blocked. Registry: `data/processed/multisource_adapter_registry.csv`; strategy: `reports/MULTISOURCE_ADAPTER_STRATEGY.md`.
## [2026-09-01] DUO-GAIT | structure correction

A direct file-level audit corrected the stale DUO-GAIT description. In both `OG_st_control` and `OG_dt_control`, the raw release contains `SA`, `LF`, and `RF` files for all 16 listed participants; the selected control RF files contain timestamp plus tri-axial acceleration columns. DUO-GAIT is therefore a conditional canonical candidate with a documented sacrum/trunk proxy, not a source missing the right foot. It still requires source-specific unit, synchronization, event, and trunk-proxy validation before pooling. The adapter registry and strategy were updated.
## [2026-09-01] DUO-GAIT | event annotation audit

The inspected `OG_st_control` trajectory JSON contains time, position, velocity, and rotation fields at approximately 128 Hz but no explicit heel-strike/toe-off event fields. DUO-GAIT therefore has complete SA/LF/RF channels but is not yet a verified full-cycle source. An event-detection adapter or recovery of the release’s event-generation procedure is required before its cycles enter healthy synthesis. Report: `reports/DUOGAIT_CYCLE_EVENT_AUDIT.md`.
## [2026-09-01] DUO-GAIT | official event method recovered

The official repository `HPI-CH/fatigue-dual-task-data` was cloned under `data/raw/duogait_2023/code/`. Its README identifies the TRIPOD-based pipeline. The local implementation confirms Tunca-style IMU event detection: gyroscope-magnitude stance thresholding with an 8-sample grace period, selected gyro-axis tilt integration with bias correction, prominence-based FO/IC search regions, and event candidate filtering. The pipeline also uses error-state trajectory estimation with zero-velocity updates and excludes turning, acceleration/deceleration, interrupted, and 3-SD outlier strides. DUO-GAIT can now proceed to official preprocessing for clean synchronized SA/LF/RF cycles. Report: `reports/DUOGAIT_EVENT_METHOD_RECOVERED.md`.
## [2026-09-01] DUO-GAIT | official full-cycle extraction completed

Using the official processed left-foot `ic_samples` tables as event timing and synchronized raw SA/LF/RF acceleration as waveform input, clean DUO-GAIT full cycles were extracted from OG_st_control. Each cycle is phase-normalized to 200 points, retains sacrum explicitly as a trunk proxy, and records its official event provenance. Outputs: `data/processed/duogait_full_gait_cycles_float32.npy` and metadata; report: `reports/DUOGAIT_FULL_CYCLE_EXTRACTION.md`.
## [2026-09-01] healthy cycles | MAREA/DUO-GAIT comparison added

Both sources now have full synchronized cycles: MAREA 5,026 cycles from 11 participants and DUO-GAIT 4,964 cycles from 16 participants. A source-distribution comparison was added before pooling, covering channel mean/standard deviation, per-cycle variability, smoothness, low-frequency power, and cycle duration. The result determines whether the generator needs source-aware conditioning rather than blind pooling.
## [2026-09-01] healthy cycles | unit mismatch found before pooling

The first MAREA/DUO-GAIT cycle comparison found a major unit mismatch: MAREA is approximately m/s² while DUO-GAIT raw acceleration is in g. DUO-GAIT mean magnitude was 1.480 versus MAREA 14.010, approximately the expected gravity conversion factor. This is a correctable adapter issue, but pooling before conversion would be invalid. DUO-GAIT will be converted by standard gravity and re-audited before generator training.
## [2026-09-01] DUO-GAIT | acceleration units harmonized

DUO-GAIT full-cycle magnitudes were converted from g to m/s² using standard gravity 9.80665. A separate harmonized cycle artifact and metadata file were created; the original raw-derived g file remains preserved. The MAREA/DUO-GAIT comparison now uses the harmonized artifact.
## [2026-09-01] MAREA + DUO-GAIT | source-conditioned generator

The source-conditioned complete-cycle diffusion generator was implemented for harmonized MAREA and DUO-GAIT cycles. Source identity is supplied as a condition during denoising; generated samples remain healthy-only and training-excluded pending realism and downstream gates.

## [2026-09-02] NONAN GaitPrint | frozen healthy-specificity score completed

The 29 predeclared, MD5-verified NONAN healthy participants were materialized
under the fixed 200-Hz mG-to-g, anti-aliased 100-Hz, LB-proxy/LF/RF magnitude
contract: 23,657 finite five-second windows. The existing
`02_ml_readiness_audit.ipynb` notebook records the full evidence and scored the
fixed CUDA canonical checkpoint exactly once, without NONAN fitting,
normalization, adaptation, calibration, threshold selection, or model
selection. At the descriptive fixed 0.50 reference there were 2/29 false
positives (specificity 93.1%; Wilson 95% CI 78.0--98.1%). The raw and isolated
spike-repaired representations had identical false-positive calls; their
maximum participant-probability difference was 0.00179. NONAN remains frozen;
this result is a healthy-specificity/domain-transport observation, not AUROC or
clinical validation.

## [2026-09-02] Notebook execution and RevalExo pipeline audit

All active (`01`--`13`) and archived (`legacy_`) notebooks were audited for
saved execution state. Every code cell has an execution count and there are no
saved error outputs; cells without rendered output are definitions/imports.
During the audit, the active RevalExo pipeline was found to retain an obsolete
gravity-removed `free_acceleration` implementation. It was replaced by the
readable, executed gravity-inclusive `acceleration` pipeline in
`11_revalexo_external_validation_pipeline.ipynb`, using canonical LB/LF/RF
order. It materialized 2,228 finite `(500, 18)` windows from 7 healthy and 10
stroke participants; RevalExo remains frozen for model selection.

## [2026-09-02] NONAN GaitPrint | false-positive review completed

The reporting-only descriptive review is executed in
`notebooks/02_ml_readiness_audit.ipynb`. It used saved frozen probabilities and
raw window summaries only; no model inference, tuning, or cohort alteration was
performed. The two false positives do not share an age group, mobility-screen
flag, artifact dependency, or a simple amplitude/dynamics profile: S043 is
high-dynamics whereas S131 is low-dynamics. The finding rules out a simplistic
global clipping/scaling response and preserves the frozen 29 participants as
reporting-only.

## [2026-09-02] NONAN GaitPrint | candidate healthy cohort materialized

The complete predeclared candidate-healthy partition was acquired and
MD5-verified: 80/80 archives, 107.32 GiB, with no partial files. The new
executed `14_nonan_candidate_healthy_materialization.ipynb` notebook verified
exact archive/partition agreement and zero overlap against frozen,
structural-audit, and mobility-screen partitions before streaming conversion.
It produced 68,398 finite `(500, 3)` LB-proxy/LF/RF magnitude windows for 80
healthy candidates; no fitting or model inference occurred. The next gate is
source-aware compatibility and bounded enrichment, while frozen NONAN and
RevalExo remain untouched.

## [2026-09-02] NONAN GaitPrint | bounded source-aware enrichment is promising, not yet admitted

The executed notebook `16_bounded_nonan_enrichment_ablation.ipynb` tested a
participant-disjoint, capped (64 windows/person) and source-downweighted
(0.10) candidate-NONAN addition after the source compatibility audit measured
high source separability (AUROC 0.8926). With fold-fitted normalization and no
access to frozen NONAN or RevalExo, mean original-source AUROC was stable
(0.9549 baseline; 0.9544 enriched), mean original-source balanced accuracy
increased (0.8157 to 0.8861), and held-out candidate healthy specificity
increased (0.9107 to 0.9630). One fold lost healthy specificity, so the cohort
is not yet admitted to final training; repeated paired evaluation is required.

## [2026-09-02] NONAN GaitPrint | repeated paired gate rejects final-training admission

Notebook `17_repeated_paired_nonan_enrichment_gate.ipynb` completed five
participant-disjoint three-fold, seed-matched baseline-versus-enriched runs.
The 15 paired resampling units showed no durable benefit: original AUROC delta
+0.0002 (bootstrap 95% CI -0.0039 to +0.0049), original healthy-specificity
delta -0.0166 (CI -0.0901 to +0.0480), and held-out candidate healthy
specificity delta -0.0021 (CI -0.0296 to +0.0283). Candidate NONAN is not
admitted to final training. Frozen NONAN and RevalExo remain untouched.

## [2026-09-02] NONAN GaitPrint | class-aware healthy alignment rejected

The GPU-executed `18_class_aware_nonan_alignment_pilot.ipynb` used a
healthy-to-healthy CORAL-style embedding loss, not blind domain alignment.
Candidate held-out healthy specificity was unchanged (0.9872), while original
balanced accuracy fell from 0.8857 to 0.8488 and original healthy specificity
fell from 0.8730 to 0.8095. The variant is rejected before repeat tuning or
frozen evaluation. Candidate NONAN remains development-only.

## [2026-09-02] Forensic source-code and sampler audit | prior conclusions narrowed

The released Felius, Voisard, and Sint code paths were compared with the local
three-magnitude pipeline. The source studies retain signed acceleration and
gyroscope information, source-specific filtering, and gait/task segmentation;
the local pool collapses them to acceleration magnitude and uses non-equivalent
window rules. Felius periodic selection is label-asymmetric (98.2% healthy
versus 78.4% stroke candidate windows retained). A literal Felius walking-
interval reproduction also excludes many irregular stroke trials, so neither
rule is sufficient by itself.

The release script and early NONAN tests used source/class window balancing
rather than participant balancing. A corrected repeated NONAN gate still did
not yield durable enrichment benefit, but this corrects the basis of the
earlier negative result. A repeated original-data benchmark showed participant
balancing improves pooled healthy specificity by +0.0690 (95% CI +0.0017 to
+0.1314), but leave-one-source-out evaluation showed poorer transport to
Voisard and Sint. A tempered participant weighting also failed its worst-source
gate. The legacy window-level sampler remains the current three-channel
reference; future work targets source-faithful gait representation rather than
more blind pooling or sampler tuning.

## [2026-09-02] Lower-back representation | gyroscope signal has directional transport value

Notebook `25_lower_back_accel_gyro_source_transport_pilot.ipynb` compared
lower-back acceleration magnitude alone with acceleration-plus-gyroscope
magnitude under Felius/Voisard leave-one-source-out training. Gyroscope
magnitude improved Felius-to-Voisard transport (mean AUROC 0.7357 to 0.8234;
healthy specificity 0.3519 to 0.4537), but not the reverse direction. This is
not a final classifier claim. It establishes that the magnitude-only contract
discarded useful lower-back dynamics and that the next technical task is a
source-faithful Sint 6-DoF adapter plus cross-source alignment audit.

## [2026-09-02] Sint lower-back 6-DoF adapter | source contract verified

The executed `26_sint_6dof_lower_back_adapter_audit.ipynb` verified all 79
published Vicon-to-Xsens mapped Sint trials (20 healthy and 10 stroke
participants). Each release-defined `lumbar` export contains finite
`Acc_X/Y/Z` and `Gyr_X/Y/Z` fields, and lumbar packets overlap each bilateral
foot export by at least 99.9%. The audit found one source-code requirement that
the older magnitude materialiser did not reproduce: the authors resample the
two GRAIL trials for healthy `900_V_01` from 40 Hz to 100 Hz; its 2MWT trial is
not in that exception. This is a correctable preprocessing defect, not a
reason to reject Sint.

`27_materialize_sint_lower_back_accel_gyro.ipynb` then created a new,
non-destructive `(4053, 500, 2)` lower-back tensor with acceleration magnitude
in g and gyroscope magnitude in deg/s: 3,069 healthy and 984 stroke windows.
It aligns packets before windowing and resamples only the two documented 40 Hz
trials. Existing three-channel tensors and all frozen cohorts were untouched.

## [2026-09-02] Lower-back 6-DoF transport | unadapted gyroscope magnitude not admitted

The GPU-executed `28_lower_back_6dof_three_source_transport_benchmark.ipynb`
ran matched leave-one-source-out comparisons across Felius, Voisard, and Sint
with three seeds, participant-level aggregation, source/class-balanced
sampling, and normalisation fit only on the two training sources. Gyroscope
added a small AUROC gain only when Felius was held out (0.850 to 0.859), while
materially worsening Sint transport (0.870 to 0.772; healthy specificity 0.750
to 0.433; Brier 0.157 to 0.287) and lowering Voisard AUROC (0.892 to 0.864).
A bounded 17 Hz acceleration / 15 Hz gyroscope low-pass variant based on the
released Sint pipeline did not resolve the Sint failure (AUROC 0.763, Brier
0.257). The result does not reject Sint or gyroscope information globally; it
rejects replacing the lower-back acceleration baseline with unadapted pooled
gyroscope magnitude at this stage. The acceleration-only lower-back track
remains the robust minimal-sensor reference, while the versioned 6-DoF tensor
is retained for future source-adapter or self-supervised work.

## [2026-09-03] Cross-dataset IMU evidence gate established

A primary-paper and official-code audit was completed before proposing another
modeling approach. BenchHAR, HAROOD, ContrastSense, CALDA/CALDG, DAGHAR,
IMUDiffusion, PPDA/WIMUSim, and recent stroke/lower-back studies were compared
against the repository's exact disease label, sensor contract, split unit, and
target-data restrictions. The audit explains why previous negative results do
not contradict the literature: many reported successes concern generic HAR or
gait detection, use target data, use different clinical preprocessing, or do
not demonstrate cross-dataset disease classification. It also confirms that
accelerometer-only input can outperform accelerometer-plus-gyroscope and that
synthetic gains can reverse for individual held-out people or larger
sensor/class configurations.

No training was run and no frozen signal was read. Future experiments now
require the checklist in [[concepts/evidence-gate-cross-dataset-imu]], including
an official-code trace, matched ERM, participant/source-disjoint validation,
training-only generator fitting, real-only validation/testing, worst-source
clinical metrics, and no RevalExo/NONAN model selection. Full report:
`../reports/EVIDENCE_GATE_CROSS_DATASET_IMU_2026-09-03.md`.

Full decision: `../reports/DATA_STORAGE_RETENTION_AUDIT_2026-09-03.md`.

After exact-path and replacement-artifact verification, 114.753 GiB was

## [2026-09-03] Model improvement | lower-back ensemble and test-set audit

Executed notebook `29_evidence_gated_source_only_domain_generalization.ipynb`
completed a GPU-backed, five-seed leave-one-source-out comparison across 314
Felius, Voisard, and Sint participants. Inner tuning was participant-disjoint,
normalization was training-only, and RevalExo/NONAN were not loaded. Standalone
CORAL and ERM++-style replacements failed the gate. A transparent
development-only rescue audit found that fixed equal averaging of ERM, CORAL,
and ERM++-style probabilities passed all non-inferiority and material-gain
conditions. Against ERM, mean AUROC improved 0.8737 to 0.8882, Brier 0.1664 to
0.1425, balanced accuracy 0.7834 to 0.8140, and specificity 0.7145 to 0.7743,
while sensitivity remained 0.8537 versus 0.8523. A secondary three-channel
confirmation also admitted an ensemble but had lower balanced accuracy and
sensitivity than the lower-back candidate.

Executed notebook `30_fp_fn_and_test_set_credibility_audit.ipynb` then recorded
seed-consensus development counts of TN=99, FP=27, FN=33, and TP=155. Felius
contributed 28 FN and Voisard contributed 15 FP. Literature and diagnostic-test
guidance confirm that this is credible development selection but not a final
clinical test. At the observed planning rates, approximately 257 independent
healthy/non-stroke and 222 independent stroke participants are needed for a
95% Wilson interval no wider than ten percentage points. See
[[concepts/evidence-gated-model-improvement]],
`../reports/MODEL_IMPROVEMENT_GATE_2026-09-03.md`, and
`../reports/TEST_SET_CREDIBILITY_AUDIT_2026-09-03.md`.

## [2026-09-03] Error reduction | threshold limit and MiniROCKET rescue rejected

Executed notebook `31_score_overlap_and_heterogeneous_rescue.ipynb` first swept
every participant-level threshold for the accepted lower-back ensemble. The
minimum total error remained 58 participants. Zero FP required 157 FN, while
zero FN required 87 FP, confirming substantial score overlap rather than a bad
cutoff. It then ran a matched three-source, five-seed lower-back MiniROCKET plus
weighted logistic classifier and a fixed 50:50 fusion with the deep ensemble.
The fusion increased mean total errors from 21.73 to 25.87 per source/seed
(19.0% worse), with mean FP +0.73 and FN +3.40. Balanced accuracy changed by
-0.0245 (95% paired bootstrap -0.0397 to -0.0089), AUROC by -0.0047, and Brier
by +0.0164. The candidate is rejected and RevalExo/NONAN were not loaded.

The next bounded model experiment is participant-level attention MIL over bags
of real lower-back windows. This addresses the weak-label mismatch created by
copying a participant diagnosis to every five-second window. The final output
remains binary. If MIL cannot reduce both error types under the same
source-held-out gate, new representative paired participants become the
limiting requirement. Full result:
`../reports/SCORE_OVERLAP_AND_HETEROGENEOUS_RESCUE_2026-09-03.md`.

## [2026-09-03] Participant-level MIL gate rejected

Executed notebook `32_participant_level_attention_mil.ipynb` compared one-loss-
per-participant mean pooling and gated-attention pooling against the selected
lower-back deep ensemble under the same three complete source holdouts and five
seeds. RevalExo and NONAN were not loaded. Mean pooling changed mean errors
from 21.73 to 21.33 (1.8% reduction), held FP at 9.80, and reduced FN from
11.93 to 11.53, but mean balanced accuracy fell from 0.8140 to 0.7691 and
AUROC from 0.8882 to 0.8442. Its paired balanced-accuracy delta was -0.0449
(95% bootstrap -0.0649 to -0.0247). Gated attention increased errors to 23.33
and degraded discrimination and calibration. Both candidates were rejected.

The error exchange was source dependent: mean pooling lowered Felius FN but
raised Felius FP, while lowering Voisard FP but sharply raising Voisard FN.
This is not robust clinical separation. The notebook 29 lower-back ensemble
remains selected and should now be frozen. New untouched paired participants,
including non-stroke gait confounders, are the limiting requirement. Full
decision: `../reports/PARTICIPANT_MIL_GATE_2026-09-03.md`.

## [2026-09-03] Exact prior-method code alignment audit

Executed notebook `33_prior_method_code_alignment_audit.ipynb` traced local
implementations against fixed official-code commits for InceptionTime,
MiniROCKET, HAROOD, ERM++, AttentionDeepMIL, GroupDRO, HAR-Bench, and IMUEval,
plus the published IMUDiffusion specification. No frozen external signal was
loaded.

The audit found that several failures had been interpreted too broadly. The
compact two-module CNN is not canonical six-module/five-network InceptionTime;
all MiniROCKET runs used 2,000 rather than 10,000 features and fixed heads;
GroupDRO weighted source×class cells rather than source domains; the ERM++
member is only an explicitly labelled subset; and the 100–200-step time-domain
DDPMs are not the 3,000-step STFT/self-attention IMUDiffusion method. CORAL's
mean-plus-covariance objective and the MIL gated-attention operator were the
closest code matches, although both retain scenario-specific adaptations.

The notebook 29 ensemble remains the empirical incumbent because its own
source-held-out evidence is valid. The only permitted corrective benchmark is
the unchanged incumbent versus canonical-mechanics lower-back InceptionTime and
canonical 10,000-feature scaled-RidgeCV MiniROCKET under the same three-source,
five-seed, participant-safe protocol. RevalExo/NONAN are excluded. Full audit:
[[concepts/prior-method-code-alignment]] and
`../reports/PRIOR_METHOD_CODE_ALIGNMENT_AUDIT_2026-09-03.md`.

## [2026-09-03] Repository navigation | first-reader notebook path

The active notebook record reached 33 files and was complete but difficult to
navigate. No evidence notebook was deleted, renamed, or moved because doing so
would break provenance links and reproducibility. A new non-notebook entry page
at `../notebooks/00_READ_THIS_FIRST.md` now defines the five-file first-reader
path (01, 02, 29, 30, 33), while `../notebooks/README.md` groups every notebook
by research phase, purpose, and current role. The only next notebook number is
34, reserved for the canonical InceptionTime/MiniROCKET corrective benchmark.

## [2026-09-03] Canonical corrective benchmark | no replacement admitted

Executed notebook `34_canonical_inceptiontime_minirocket_corrective_benchmark.ipynb`
completed the final bounded architecture correction on 22,506 lower-back
windows from 314 Felius, Voisard, and Sint participants. It used all three
complete source holdouts, five fixed seeds, participant-level aggregation,
training-only preprocessing/calibration, and no RevalExo or NONAN signal.

The notebook-29 incumbent remained best at mean AUROC 0.8882, Brier 0.1425,
balanced accuracy 0.8140, FP 9.80, FN 11.93, and 21.73 total participant errors
per source/seed evaluation. InceptionTime canonical mechanics increased total
errors to 32.13. Canonical 10k MiniROCKET increased them to 23.27. The closest
fixed incumbent/MiniROCKET fusion slightly raised balanced accuracy to 0.8145
and lowered FP to 9.33, but raised FN to 12.80 and total errors to 22.13. It
helped Voisard by 2.4 errors but added 3.0 errors in Felius and 0.6 in Sint.

No candidate met the predeclared requirement to reduce both FP and FN, lower
total errors by at least 10%, preserve every held-out source, and retain
discrimination/calibration. Architecture rotation on the same 314 participants
is now closed. The selected lower-back ensemble remains frozen, and a new
untouched paired cohort is the next evidence requirement. Full report:
`../reports/CANONICAL_CORRECTIVE_BENCHMARK_2026-09-03.md`.
